**The full-import argument was weaker than it first looked, and this corrects an earlier framing in this file.** "Catch importer bugs across the region" is largely already banked: the importer runs clean across all 421 (`no assertion failures`), and `[M27C C5]`'s reachability audit covered all **1294 warps region-wide**, not merely the baked ones. What a full bake would add is exercising the BAKE stage for the 40 unrendered atlas pairs — real, but bounded. Separately retired: the claim that the import should happen "while the guards still mean something" (see the M27M block's own scene-as-truth note). **Git is the recovery mechanism and always was** — a `--force` bake that clobbers hand-painted work is recoverable by checkout, so `_scene_divergence` and `check_bake_diff` protect only the narrow window of UNCOMMITTED edits, which is the same window every other file in this project already lives in. What is actually lost at the flip is an early-warning signal ("which of these files no longer matches what the importer would produce"), and that signal was going to drown in legitimate divergence regardless of timing.

**Consequence for sequencing**: M27D/F/G/H are built against the corridor and widened afterwards, rather than each being built against the whole region.

**[M27D Step 0 — Entities & NPCs] SCOPED 2026-07-30. ⚠️ Scope of record is `docs/m27d_recon.md`** — read that, not this paragraph, before building.

**226 entities are placed across the corridor and not one does anything.** `npc.gd` is 37 lines of `@export`s and a configuration warning; there is no sprite, no movement, no occupancy, no interaction.

**Three measurements reshaped the sizing.** (1) **The movement problem is far smaller than it looks**: source defines 89 `MOVEMENT_TYPE_*`, the corridor uses **11**, and they collapse to **four behaviours** — of which **96 of 132 entities never move at all**. Sizing this block against `event_object_movement.c`'s 12,245 lines sizes it against the wrong problem; that file covers berry trees, cycling, surfing and 78 types the corridor never names. (2) **No overworld sprites exist in this project at all** — 449 in source, 46 distinct ids needed, resolved through a **four-hop chain** (pointer table → info struct → pic table → INCBIN) that is not derivable from the id by any string transform. (3) **Nothing models entity occupancy**, so you currently walk straight through every NPC.

**Two sprite exceptions found by checking rather than assuming**, both of which a flat copy would have shipped wrong: `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_WORKER_M`'s embedded palette does NOT match its tagged `npc_white.pal` (44 of 46 do), the same shape as Phase 5b's Thunder — so a pull must resolve `.paletteTag` per sprite; and `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_VAR_0` is **not a sprite**, it is a script-variable-chosen appearance resolved by `VarGetObjectEventGraphicsId`, so it cannot be pulled at all and needs M27G.

**Two pieces of real work are already banked in the EDITOR and want moving rather than rewriting**: `MapOverlay.trainer_sight_cells()` walks `StepResolver.resolve()` (so a sight line can never disagree with the movement rules it describes), and `_sight_blocking_cells()` already records the occupancy rule — only `npc`/`trainer`/`item` block, because `DoesObjectCollideWithObjectAt` consults the `object_events` array and nothing else.

**The battle seam exists on ONE SIDE ONLY.** `BattleSetupContext.set_pending(...)` is consumed by `battle_screen.gd` already and `set_trainer_data()` is wired, so the overworld can START a battle today. There is no way BACK — no result object, no restore-where-you-left-it, no defeated-trainer flag — and **no player party anywhere in the overworld**. This is the seam the vertical slice exists to prove.

**Four decisions resolved 2026-07-30, detail in that doc's §5-6.** (1) **Debug party for the pre-alpha**, widening to a real one as the slice grows — D5 proves the seam without waiting on M27K. (2) **`OBJ_EVENT_GFX_VAR_*` adopts source's own fallback**: 44 region-wide across all 8 slots, and `GetObjectEventGraphicsInfo` already ends `if (graphicsId >= NUM_OBJ_EVENT_GFX) graphicsId = OBJ_EVENT_GFX_NINJA_BOY` — a visible real sprite, no invented placeholder, and M27G's VAR lookup slots in ahead of it with nothing to undo. (3) **An empty `movement_type` is source behaviour, not a defect** — 9 region-wide, 6 of them cuttable trees, and source's absent value is `MOVEMENT_TYPE_NONE`. ⚠️ **The finding underneath matters more: HM OBSTACLES ARE OBJECT EVENTS, NOT TILES** — 210 region-wide (97 breakable rocks, 55 cuttable trees, 58 pushable boulders) — so **D2 makes every one of them solid for free** under the existing `npc`/`trainer`/`item` rule, and **M27E is partly an entity problem**, worth knowing before that block is scoped. (4) **Build the flag/var store in D4, in memory, shaped for serialisation** — source remembers a beaten trainer as one bit (`HasTrainerBeenFought` = `FlagGet(TRAINER_FLAGS_START + id)`), and **this project already IMPORTS the data for flags and vars but reads neither**: 31 of the corridor's 132 object events carry a visibility flag and 30 triggers reference 9 distinct VARs. One store answers three needs — beaten trainers, the 31 NPCs currently drawn unconditionally, and M27G's whole substrate — so an ad-hoc "defeated" set would be the same work done twice.

⚠️ **D1 SCOPE WIDENED (Rob): pull the WHOLE object-event sprite set, not the 46 the corridor names.** Measured **449 PNGs totalling 0.5 MB** across **387 graphics ids**, of which only **26 are `_FRLG`** — the other 361 are the Hoenn/Emerald set Rob wants available later. At half a megabyte a selective pull saves nothing and costs a second session, and this project has already run that experiment: `[M26B3-1]` pulled 93 trainer front pics and the Kanto roster forced a second pull of 62 more. The generated id→sheet table should cover all 387 for the same reason, so a map baked later never meets an id the table does not know.

**D1 also absorbs three small pulls (Rob, 2026-07-30), ~55 KB total, detail in §7.** **Not optional**: `emotion_exclamation.png` (D4 has no visual for its own trigger without it) and `shadow_small/medium/large.png` (`.shadowSize` is real per-sprite data on **all 393** graphics-info entries — 324 M, 67 S, 1 L, 1 NONE — so a shadow is part of the sprite record, not decoration); the player's overworld sprite comes free inside the 449, replacing the red `ColorRect`. **Free and already wanted**: all **66 field effects, 37 KB** — grass rustle for M27H, `cut_grass`/`surf_blob`/`rock_climb_*` for M27E, and `shiny_sparkle` for M27's own flagged shiny gap. **Closes an open gap**: the **25** trainer front pics source has and this project does not (180 vs 155), retiring `[M26B3-1]`'s dangling-stem counter rather than leaving it armed. **Battle-side Pokémon art needs nothing** — 387 front / 387 back / 386 icons already pulled. ⚠️ **None of these files carry tRNS and index 0 is the transparency key**, so a plain copy renders each inside an opaque box, exactly as the ball sheets did in `[M26B3-6a]`; tag the INDEX, since index 0 is not a constant colour across them.

Proposed **D1** sprite pull + static render · **D2** occupancy (the one change that touches the movement core, so early while the corridor is the only consumer) · **D3** the four movement behaviours · **D4** sight + approach · **D5** the battle round trip. **Four decisions for Rob in that doc's §5**, the load-bearing one being whether D5 uses a debug party or pulls M27K's starter choice forward.

**[M27D D1 — the overworld sprite set] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. Pallet Town is populated.**

New `gen_object_event_sprites.py` pulls **449 object-event sprites + 66 field effects + the 25 missing trainer front pics** (portraits **155 → 180**, retiring `[M26B3-1]`'s dangling-stem counter) and generates `ObjectEventGraphics` — **385 of 387 ids** with sheet, frame size and frame count. The player is no longer a red `ColorRect`; it draws Leaf through the same builder as every NPC, matching the battle side's own `_PLAYER_BACK_PIC` placeholder so the two halves agree on who the player is until M27K.

⚠️ **THREE BUGS THE PULL WOULD HAVE SHIPPED, EACH FOUND BY CHECKING RATHER THAN ASSUMING.**

**1. Sheets are HORIZONTAL strips and the first cut read them vertically.** Measured across every resolved id: **384 horizontal, 0 vertical**. This renders SOUTH perfectly — frame 0 sits at the origin either way — and reads off the image for the other three facings. **The first screenshot showed two resting NPCs and looked completely correct.** AL.04 now checks the layout across every sheet rather than sampling.

**2. Nine basenames collided across 20 files.** `walking.png` alone is shared by brendan, may and both Ruby/Sapphire variants, so a basename copy silently overwrote all but the last — **four player characters collapsing into one sprite**. Same shape as `[M27C]`'s Route 22 node-name collision: a name derived from partial data, wrong only where two things meet. Stems now flatten the full path below `pics/`, with an assertion that the rule stays injective. Fixing it took the sheet count 347 → 358.

**3. The pic-table name is not derivable from the info symbol.** `gObjectEventGraphicsInfo_Azumarill` points at `sPicTable_AzumarillOld`; deriving cost **48 of 387 ids**. The struct has an explicit `.images` field — read it. Same guess-instead-of-read mistake M27A already paid for with tileset directories. Pic tables also live in **four files**, not one (berry trees and followers have their own).

**7 sprites carry a palette that differs from the PNG they ship in**, `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_WORKER_M` among them — **9 of its 16 entries differ and 5 of those indices are used by real pixels**, so a flat copy renders it green and pink. Each sprite gets its `.paletteTag` applied and the count is REPORTED, because a rising number means the reference changed. Index 0 is also tagged transparent on all 515 files: no source file carries tRNS, and a plain copy draws every sprite inside an opaque box, exactly as the ball sheets did in `[M26B3-6a]`.

**A verification method was itself broken, which is worth more than the bug it hid.** `ImageChops.difference(a, b).getbbox() is None` reported two clearly different frames as identical — **`getbbox()` on RGBA keys off the ALPHA channel**, and both frames share an alpha mask, so it answers "empty" however much the colours differ. It flagged 27 sheets as having no back view and sent a real investigation down a false trail. Compare `tobytes()`. Redone correctly: **208 of 210 people sheets have three genuinely distinct facing frames** (the 2 exceptions are a sitting boy and a watering pose).

**Deliberately absent**: `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_VAR_0..F` (44 region-wide, resolved from a script variable at runtime — nothing to pull until M27G) and `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_OW_MON` (the follower, chosen by species). Both fall back to source's own `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_NINJA_BOY`, a real visible sprite rather than an invented placeholder.

Sprites are built with **no `owner`**, so Godot never serialises them and a baked scene stays byte-identical whether or not it has been opened — 32/32 still reproducible, which matters because `check_bake_diff` would otherwise read 32 maps as hand-edited.

**Tests.** Section **AL**, 14 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **425 → 439**. AL.04 is the roster sweep for the layout bug; AL.08 and AL.14 are split so a layout defect and a missing mirror report separately rather than one assertion covering both. Both verified by reintroducing each break. AL.12 initially failed by being too strict — it caught the corridor's real `VAR_0` NPC — and now asserts the exception explicitly rather than being loosened.

**[M27D D2 — entity occupancy] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. NPCs are solid.**

`StepResolver` gained a fifth cell-source method, `entity_at`, and a new `Outcome.OBJECT_EVENT`. **The rule is source's, not a choice**: `DoesObjectCollideWithObjectAt` consults the `object_events` array and nothing else, so `npc`/`trainer`/`item_ball` block while warps, triggers and signs occupy no collision slot — which is precisely why a trainer sees over a doormat and why you can stand on one.

⚠️ **THE CHECK GOES LAST, AFTER ELEVATION**, matching `GetVanillaCollision`'s own precedence (range → terrain/directional → elevation → object event). Walking at an NPC standing on a different stratum therefore reports `ELEVATION_MISMATCH`, not `OBJECT_EVENT`. Both block, so **nothing in play can tell the two apart** — only a test can, which is why AM.03 exists.

**The 210 HM obstacles became solid for free** (97 breakable rocks, 55 cuttable trees, 58 pushable boulders). They import as `npc` kind, so the existing rule covers them with no obstacle-specific code — the consequence flagged when the empty-`movement_type` question was answered.

`MapData.entity_at()` returns false **and that is correct rather than a stub**: entities are scene NODES while `MapData` is the per-cell terrain resource, so the editor overlay's own view genuinely has nobody standing on it. Occupancy is a per-chunk SET rebuilt at install rather than a per-step scan of the scene tree — a step already costs a resolve, and walking every entity of every live chunk per step is the same per-cell cost that made C4's skirt repaint 4000 dictionary walks. It is also the shape D3 needs once NPCs move.

**Not yet gated on `visibility_flag`** — source hides a flagged-away object event and stops it colliding; nothing reads flags until the store lands in D4, so every placed entity currently blocks. Recorded rather than silently assumed.

**Tests.** Section **AM**, 9 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **439 → 448**. Precedence is tested against a synthetic cell source, because `MapData` always answers false and the corridor may not happen to place an NPC on a mismatched stratum.

⚠️ **One assertion was written vacuous and caught by trying to break it — the third time this arc.** "Unloading releases every cell it was blocking" passed with the release REMOVED, because `entity_at()` asks `chunk_owning()` first and that already answers `""` for an unloaded chunk, so a stale set is unreachable through the public path. The leak is real regardless — a warp unloads every chunk, so a session accumulates one dead set per map entered — so it is now asserted against the manager's own state instead. **A second break was more informative than its own assertion**: letting warps and signs block breaks four WARP tests (AH.06, AI.03, AI.04, AJ.03), because you can no longer step onto a warp tile — the occupancy rule turns out to be load-bearing for warp dispatch, which nothing had recorded.

**[M27D D3 — NPC movement] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. The corridor is alive.**

**Source's four behaviours are ONE state machine with two parameters** — which direction set, and whether choosing a direction also steps. `MovementType_LookAround_Step4` and `MovementType_FaceDownAndUp_Step4` differ only in the table they `memcpy`; `MovementType_WanderAround_Step4` differs only in going on to walk. Reproducing four behaviours would have been reproducing the table layout rather than the mechanic. A blocked wander turns without moving, which is source's own `if (GetCollisionInDirection(...)) sTypeFuncId = 1`.

⚠️ **`movement_range_x/y` existed in source's map data and this project was not importing it.** Without it a wandering NPC walks until terrain happens to stop it, which is a different map. Now extracted, baked and honoured — and the semantics are the trap: it is a **HALF-EXTENT from the spawn cell, per axis, where 0 means UNCONSTRAINED**, not a zero-size box. `IsCoordOutsideObjectEventMovementRange` skips the check entirely for a zero range, and most NPCs carry 0 on at least one axis, so reading it as a box would have pinned much of the region in place. AN.07 pins it; breaking it fails immediately.

Delays are source's `sMovementDelaysMedium` — `{32, 64, 96, 128}` frames — **converted to seconds**, because source is 60fps-locked and this project is not: `[M26G4]` measured frame-tied stepping running ~10% slow at 144Hz and half speed at 30Hz.

**Movement is driven by `MapManager`, not by each NPC's own `_process`**, because moving one is not a private act: the destination must clear terrain, the wander box AND every other entity, and the occupancy set has to stay true afterwards. An NPC cannot answer any of that alone, and a back-reference per entity would be the same coupling in 100 copies. Every wanderer walks through the **same resolver the player does**, so it obeys ledges, directional tiles and elevation identically. Occupancy is updated incrementally rather than rebuilt — a per-NPC-per-step rebuild is the per-cell cost C4 already paid for once with the skirt repaint.

⚠️ **THAT DISCLOSED RACE DOES NOT EXIST, and measuring it found a real bug instead.** The note here claimed two NPCs ticking in one frame could both take a cell "because the occupancy set only updates after a move". Wrong: `move_entity` runs synchronously inside the tick loop, so a later NPC in the same frame already sees the cell taken — verified directly. The `reserved` set is belt-and-braces, not the thing preventing it.

**What the check DID find: NPCs walked straight through the player.** The player is not a placed entity — spawned by the controller, not baked into a map — so it was absent from the occupancy set entirely. Measured: the player's own cell reported `entity_at` false and a step into it resolved to `NONE`. Fixed by giving the manager the player's cell, so an NPC is refused for the same reason another NPC refuses it (`OBJECT_EVENT`) rather than by a player-specific special case.

⚠️ **The first fix introduced a drift bug, and the tests caught it.** Notifying the manager from four call sites left `_cell` and the manager's copy as one fact stored twice; a test helper set `_cell` directly without notifying, and the manager went on blocking the square the player had LEFT — **a phantom body that broke two unrelated warp tests**. `_cell` is now a SETTER that notifies, making the drift unrepresentable rather than something to remember at each new call site. AO.03 pins it.

**Still latent, and genuinely not reachable today**: an NPC crossing a chunk seam would have its occupancy written into the wrong chunk's set, because `move_entity` keys on the NPC's own map. Measured: **0 NPCs in the corridor are both unconstrained on an axis and within one cell of their map edge**, so nothing can currently reach it. Recorded rather than pre-solved.

Verified live over 8 seconds on the corridor: **4 wanderers moved, the fixed-facing NPC never did, 0 left their range, occupancy correct throughout, no two NPCs sharing a cell.**

⚠️ **A first run reported one NPC escaping its range, and the bug was in the MEASUREMENT.** The driver recorded each "spawn" two frames after load — by which point NPCs had already ticked and moved — so it compared against a post-move cell. Re-measured against each NPC's own recorded spawn: zero escapes. Worth recording because a wrong measurement that accuses correct code costs the same as a real bug until you check which it is.

**Tests.** Sections **AN** (14) and **AO** (4), `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **448 → 466**. Four breaks verified: reading range 0 as a box fails AN.07, letting look-around step fails AN.11, forgetting to free the cell an entity left fails AN.13, and dropping the player notification fails AO.01/AO.02/AO.04.

**[M27D perf — first-visit hitches] PARTIAL, and the honest breakdown matters more than the fix.** Rob: *"little hitches when transitioning to a map for the first time."*

**Profiled the live crossing rather than a synthetic install**, per C4's own lesson about measuring the wrong thing. Per-phase, cold:

| | |
|---|---|
| resource parse | **30-36 ms** — already OFF the main thread, `threaded_get` measures 0.0 ms |
| skirt repaint | **4.8 ms** |
| instantiate + `add_child` (all entity `_ready()`, sprites included) | **1.7 ms** |
| `tick_entities` (all NPC movement) | **0.03-0.2 ms** |

**So D1-D3 are not the cause.** Sprites and NPC movement are noise; the cost is Godot parsing a 68 KB scene of which **89% is base64 tile data**.

**Fixed: the skirt spike.** `_skirt_queue` held RECTS, and one rect repainted EVERY affected chunk in a single call — each chunk being 4-7k `set_cell` calls (2256 cells x 3 planes for Viridian City). Now a queue of chunk NAMES, one per frame, newly-installed chunk first — because its own tiles are what a neighbour's stale skirt is painted over, so that ordering shortens the visible seam rather than merely spreading cost. Verified no regression: **0 unpainted cells across all three planes**.

**Rob: still hitching. Second pass found the real cause, and RETRACTED a claim from the first.**

⚠️ **THE "SCENE PARSING IS THE COST" CONCLUSION WAS WRONG, and so was the 37x binary-format figure that followed it.** That measurement compared `.tscn` against `.scn` with the shared TileSet already warm in the resource cache, so it timed the text parse alone and attributed the whole load to it. A binary twin was built, measured in the real path, found NOT to help, and **reverted** — adding 32 files and a drift hazard for an unproven gain is exactly what should not ship. `.tscn` remains the only artifact.

**The real cost, measured repeatedly: a cold shared TileSet is ~30 ms**, and it is paid the FIRST time any map using that tileset PAIR is loaded. That is precisely "hitches when transitioning to a map for the first time", and it explains why the hitch does not recur for the same region afterwards. It also matches M27M2's own independently-derived ~27 ms for building one (2208 `create_tile()` calls) — a figure already in this file, describing the same object, which the first pass walked straight past.

**Fixed by warming all of them at boot**, off-thread and deliberately NOT awaited: **14 pairs back the 32-map corridor**, 60 the region, so the cost is bounded and paid once somewhere a pause is expected. A map reached before its tileset finishes simply pays what it pays today, making the worst case the current behaviour rather than a new stall. Measured: a first-visit `PewterCity_Frlg` load **34-42 ms → 11.5 ms**.

⚠️ **Honest limit on all of this: these numbers come from a machine running llvmpipe software rendering**, where a baseline frame is ~8 ms and `TIME_PROCESS` attribution put this project's own per-frame code at **0.07 ms of 7.9 ms**. The relative costs transfer; the absolute ones do not.

⚠️ **A measurement nearly reported a false alarm, again.** The skirt-coverage check found "90 of 180 cells unpainted" — because it read only `BorderSkirt_Ground`, and C3 already established that a border metatile routes to one or TWO of three planes, so roughly half the cells legitimately have nothing on ground. Checked across all three: zero holes. Third time this arc a broken check accused correct code.

**[M27D D4 — trainer sight, approach, and the flag/var store] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. A trainer notices you and walks over.**

New `scripts/overworld/flag_store.gd`. Source keys persistent state by numeric id — `HasTrainerBeenFought` is `FlagGet(TRAINER_FLAGS_START + trainerId)`, 0x500 + id (`battle_setup.c:1287`) — but this project retired `trainer_id` at `[M24a]` because it is a sorted-alphabetical index that shifts on regen. **So the store is NAME-keyed**, against the constants the importer already carries: `FLAG_*` on 31 of the corridor's object events, `VAR_*` on 30 triggers, and a derived `DEFEATED_<key>` per trainer. One store answers all three, and it is shaped for M27L — both backing dictionaries are already `String -> bool` / `String -> int`.

**Two source behaviours that read backwards if guessed.** `visibility_flag` means HIDDEN, not shown — every one of source's own is named `FLAG_HIDE_*`, so inverting it would display exactly the entities meant to be gone (break-tested: AP.12/AP.13 both fail on the flip). And `FlagGet` returns FALSE for an unknown id rather than erroring, so storing only the SET flags is the faithful shape, not a shortcut. One deliberate divergence, recorded at the field: `VarGet` returns the *id itself* when there is no backing pointer, a trick for making the 0x8000+ range work as script immediates — with name keys there is no id to return, so M27G resolves those by name at the call site instead.

⚠️ **THE SIGHT LINE USES THE LIVE FACING, AND THE EDITOR'S COPY DOES NOT.** `MapOverlay.trainer_sight_cells` draws rays only for FIXED-facing trainers, which is right for a static editor view and would be wrong here: source reads `trainerObj->facingDirection` (`trainer_see.c:636`), so a rotating trainer genuinely sees whichever way it is currently turned. Moving the overlay's code verbatim would have **blinded the 97 rotating and 74 walking trainers in Kanto — 39.6% of the roster** — while looking correct on the 261 fixed ones. AQ.10/AQ.11 pin it.

**`sight_reaches_player` collapses five source functions into one.** The four directional distance functions are the same test with the comparison operators permuted (same axis, strictly ahead, within range), so they are one expression rather than four transcriptions. `CheckPathBetweenTrainerAndPlayer` then walks the cells STRICTLY BETWEEN trainer and player through `StepResolver.resolve()` — which is what makes directional impassability, elevation mismatch and another object event each break the line without restating any of it, matching source masking out only `COLLISION_OUTSIDE_RANGE`. Source's final step is a collision check at the player's own cell requiring `COLLISION_OBJECT_EVENT` — that is how it confirms the player is really standing there; here the ray is derived from the player's cell, so it holds by construction.

**The returned distance is 1-BASED and that is load-bearing.** `CheckTrainer` hands `InitTrainerApproachTask` a range of `approachDistance - 1`, so a trainer three tiles away walks two and stops ADJACENT. Walking the full distance would put it on the player's own cell.

**Ordering is data, not scene-tree order** — `CheckForTrainersWantingBattle` sorts candidates by `localId` before walking them, so which of two simultaneous trainers gets the battle is decided by the map, reproduced in `trainers_seeing_player()`.

**Sight fires on STEP COMPLETION, the same seam warps use**, and for the same source reason (the field-control step hook, not a per-frame poll). That is also what makes it safe: a player standing inside a line is checked once per arrival and cannot re-trigger while still.

**`MapManager` answers GEOMETRY ONLY.** Whether a seeing trainer should actually battle — beaten, hidden — is `overworld.gd`'s question, because the manager has no game state and giving it one to answer that would be the wrong seam. Source skips a beaten trainer and keeps scanning, so a second trainer behind a beaten one still gets its battle; reproduced.

**Verified live on real corridor data**, not just in fixtures: `TRAINER_LASS_ROBIN_FRLG` on Route 3 at (40,12) facing south, range 3, player at (40,15) — reported distance 3, walked **(40,12) → (40,14)**, i.e. two tiles, stopping adjacent; the player turned to face back down the line; and after `set_trainer_defeated` the same position produced no second sighting.

**Tests.** Sections **AP** (19, flag/var store) and **AQ** (14, sight geometry), `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **466 → 499** (500 reported with Z.99). AQ is synthetic throughout and deliberately so — a geometry rule needs cases chosen to DISCRIMINATE (off-axis, out of range, behind, walled, blocked by a body, `sight_range` 0) rather than whichever the corridor happens to contain. Both headline guards were proven to catch their regressions before being trusted: skipping the intermediate-cell walk fails AQ.07/AQ.09, and inverting the visibility polarity fails AP.12/AP.13. `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32 still reproducible.

**Deliberately NOT built:** the battle handoff itself (D5), and source's `TRAINER_TYPE_SEE_ALL_DIRECTIONS`/`BURIED` branches — zero Kanto trainers use either, confirmed at `[M27B]`.

**[M27D perf — boot-time TileSet preload] COMPLETE — 2026-07-30. The first-visit hitch is gone.**

**Step 0 measured before anything was written**, per the MEASURED-CONSTANT RULE: one pair, fresh process, full load path including PNG decode — **16.4 ms steady, 23.6 ms with a cold OS file cache**. A second load in the same process cost the SAME (16.27 vs 16.49), which says plainly that **PNG decode is not the dominant term — tile construction is**, consistent with the trimmed-TileSet recon's own finding. Disk inventory: **14 `.tres` pairs, exactly the 14 the 32 baked corridor scenes reference** — zero unreferenced, zero missing.

`MapManager.preload_tilesets()` loads every pair once at boot and **holds the reference** in a static Dictionary. Static deliberately: a `MapManager` is a scene node and dies with its scene (a battle, a title screen, any `change_scene_to_file`), and residence across all of that is the whole point — the same class-level-static shape `BattleSetupContext` already uses, needing no autoload. **Holding the ref is the mechanism, not decoration**: `ResourceLoader`'s cache can evict an entry with no live reference, and then the next first visit silently pays full price again.

**This SUPERSEDES the threaded `warm_tilesets`**, which fired `load_threaded_request` and kept no reference — so it neither guaranteed residence nor reported whether it had worked. Keeping both would have been two mechanisms for one job, the duplication shape that already cost this project a `check_bake_diff` false positive.

**Moved AHEAD of `load_chunk(start_map)`** in `overworld.gd:_ready`, so the start map's own pair is not the one pair still paying full price.

**Arithmetic guard, Z.99-style**: zero files found, or loaded != found, reports loudly. ⚠️ **The break test forced a real design point**: `run_overworld_tests.sh` fails a run on any engine `ERROR:` line — correctly, since a stray error means a test function aborted silently — so a test that must PROVE the guard fires cannot let it `push_error`. Added a `strict` testing seam (default true; false records the message in `last_diagnostic` instead), matching the `_force_hit`/`_force_roll` precedent. The test then asserts on the TEXT — that it names both the condition and the directory — which is strictly stronger than asserting a return of 0, and AR.14 pins that a healthy run leaves the diagnostic EMPTY, without which AR.12 would prove nothing.

**Results.** Boot: **14 pairs, 285-294 ms**, slowest `building_frlg__pokemon_center_frlg` at 28.6 ms. First-visit resolve of a pair afterwards: **0.008 ms** (was 16.4-23.6). A second pair's first touch: 0.002 ms. Work is RELOCATED, not reduced — this is explicitly NOT the M27M trim; no tile definitions, atlases or `.tres` content changed. Region-wide the same pass would be ~1.2 s for 60 pairs, which is the number to watch when the full import lands (the trim recon's own 4.0x would cut it to ~300 ms).

**Tests.** Section **AR**, 14 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **499 → 513** (514 with Z.99), gated on the tileset directory existing so a fresh checkout still balances. Covers exact-count-against-disk (not "at least one"), same-instance-on-repeat-lookup (a fresh instance per call would mean nothing was retained), and **a genuinely exercised fallback** — the baker returns the held instance while preloaded, and still resolves from disk with the dictionary cleared, so neither path is decorative. `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32, untouched as expected. ⚠️ **Not verified by feel** — Rob's manual pass on the Pallet Town → Route 1 edge from a fresh boot is what confirms the hitch is actually gone.

**[M27F Stages 1 & 2 — interaction, dialogue, and the script engine driving the battle engine] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. You can talk to Kanto, and Brock is beatable end to end.**

**Stage 1 — press A and something happens.** New `Interaction.resolve()` ports `GetInteractionScript`'s dispatch order (object event → background event; metatile and water are later stages, and their sitting BEHIND object events is why the 31 `readable` behaviours only fire on a tile nothing else claims). ⚠️ **THE COUNTER HOP is the load-bearing part**: if the faced tile is `MB_COUNTER` the search moves ONE TILE FURTHER (`ProcessPlayerFieldInput`), which is 729 cells across 89 corridor maps — without it every Poké Mart clerk, nurse and gym receptionist is unreachable, while everything outdoors reads as correct. New `MessageBox` (CanvasLayer 50) owns a `TextTyper` **vendored out of the Dialogue Manager plugin** rather than duck-typing into `DialogueLabel`'s private seam; the plugin is no longer used by anything. New `ScriptVM` exposes `pc`/`current_op`/`pause_reason` as plain readable properties rather than state trapped in a suspended coroutine — which is what later let a battle park a script across an overlay takeover.

⚠️ **THE CONDITIONAL FAMILY IS TWENTY OPCODES, AND THE LABEL IS THE LAST ARGUMENT.** `goto_if_eq a, b, c` expands to `trycompare goto_if, 1, a, b, c` (`asm/macros/event.inc:2023`) — it carries its own comparison and the jump target is `args[2]`. The first cut read `args[0]`, so every gated script in the region jumped to a VAR NAME and died UNRESOLVED; found by talking to Pallet Town's Sign Lady. Three argument shapes, all confirmed: comparison (3-arg self-comparing, or 1-arg leaning on a preceding `compare`), flag (`goto_if_set flag, dest` → `checkflag` + `goto_if TRUE`, :1994), and trainer-defeated (`checktrainerflag`, :2095) — times `goto_`/`call_` prefixes. **`compare` had been deleted as "never emitted"; the corpus has exactly 33 `compare` ops and exactly 33 one-argument conditionals.** That pairing is how the deletion was caught, and `case` depends on it.

**Stage 2 — `trainerbattle_single`.** The first opcode whose pause outlives its own frame and whose RESULT decides where the script goes next (new `Pause.WAIT_BATTLE`). ⚠️ **THE ARITY SELECTS THE BATTLE TYPE** (:787): 3 args is `TRAINER_BATTLE_SINGLE` (**753 uses**), 4–5 is `TRAINER_BATTLE_CONTINUE_SCRIPT` (**114**), so implementing either alone is wrong for most of Kanto. ⚠️ **AND THERE ARE TWO DIFFERENT CONTINUATION POINTS, not one**: an already-beaten trainer resolves through `gotopostbattlescript` → `BattleSetup_GetScriptAddrAfterBattle` = the calling script's NEXT opcode, while a win resolves through `gotobeatenscript` → `BattleSetup_GetTrainerPostBattleScript` = the event_script ARGUMENT. The already-beaten skip is not in the command at all — it is `specialvar VAR_RESULT, GetTrainerFlag` inside the shared `EventScript_TryDoNormalTrainerBattle`, one level below, which is why Brock's own script carries no guard and would otherwise re-challenge forever.

**Deliberate divergence, recorded at the code site**: source's `ScrCmd_trainerbattle` jumps to that shared standard script and the real logic lives there as script DATA. It is unrunnable here (it needs `applymovement`, `special` and `specialvar`, all later stages), so its DECISION STRUCTURE is reproduced natively; its other content is presentation this project either already does (D4 walks the trainer over) or has no equivalent for (encounter music). New guard: a plain `resume()` **cannot** clear `WAIT_BATTLE`, because doing so would silently skip the win/loss branch and read as "the post-battle script just did not run".

**Also shipped**: `famechecker` (no-op), `setflag`/`clearflag`/`setvar`, plus `switch`/`case`/`copyvar`/`settrainerflag`/`set_gym_trainers_frlg`. That last group is scope deliberately widened past the agreed line — without it Brock's win path stopped one opcode short of its own reward chain. `switch`/`case` alone are **2,529 corpus uses**, second only to the conditionals, and `case` expands to `compare` + the one-argument `goto_if_eq`.

⚠️ **TWO REAL BUGS FOUND BY LIVE-DRIVING, NEITHER CATCHABLE BY THE SUITE.** (1) **Every trainer battle in Kanto was unstartable.** `gen_map_scripts.py` never canonicalised trainer keys — scripts carry the bare source constant `TRAINER_LEADER_BROCK` while the roster this project generates is keyed `TRAINER_LEADER_BROCK_FRLG`, so the party lookup found nothing and the battle silently refused, which reads as "the script did not run". **This is [M27B Step 5]'s fix recurring**: that session routed PLACEMENTS through `canonical_key()`; this compiler was written later and never got the same treatment. Fixed at the compiler with a new `is_trainer_constant()` in `trainer_keys.py`, so the suffix rule keeps exactly one home (Rule A) — `canonical_key()` RAISES on unknown, and a `TRAINER_*` argument is often a battle-MODE constant (`TRAINER_BATTLE_SINGLE`) rather than a trainer, so a membership test was genuinely needed. Only what the index recognises is transformed. (2) **A double-battle race in production code**: `_on_battle_overlay_finished` cleared `_in_battle` BEFORE applying the result, leaving the whole fade-in as a window in which `_drive_script` saw the still-parked `WAIT_BATTLE` and started a SECOND battle. Symptom was precise — beaten flag set, badge not, overlay back on screen. The result is now applied while the guard still holds.

⚠️ **A SPOTTED TRAINER BYPASSED THEIR OWN SCRIPT.** `_run_trainer_approach` called `start_trainer_battle(t)` directly, never touching `t.script_label` — so every route trainer in the corridor battled in total silence and never ran their post-battle branch, meaning `goto_if_defeated` never fired for any of them. Invisible because the battle itself worked. Now the approach hands off to the trainer's own script, matching source (the approach falls through to it), with a fallback to a bare battle if the script is absent or unrunnable. Live-verified in Viridian Forest: Bug Catcher Rick spots the player, walks over, **speaks**, then battles.

**Brock, live, in the real scene**: talk → 7 pages of real intro speech → battle mounts → win → `FLAG_BADGE01_GET` set, `VAR_MAP_SCENE_PEWTER_CITY = 1`, Camper Liam pre-marked beaten → post-battle chain runs to `GiveTM39` and stops at **`checkitemspace`**. Talking again starts no second battle and lands on the same opcode. Both paths converge on one genuine blocker: the bag, which is **M27I**.

**Corpus coverage 18.6% → 27.5%** of all 17,138 labels running to completion. Next blockers: `applymovement` **7.7%**, `special` **5.0%** (not one opcode — a few hundred named C functions, and it still wants its own scoping session). ⚠️ **Caveat on those figures: the indexer sweeps in battle-ANIMATION scripts too** (`monbg`/`createsprite`/`createvisualtask` ≈ 6.5%), so real map-script coverage is better than 27.5% and that indexer scope is worth its own look.

**Tests**: `m27f_script_vm_test` 41 → **88/88** (sections G conditionals, H interaction, I Stage 2 incl. three against Brock's real compiled script), `m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514, `check_bake_diff` 32/32 reproducible. Every headline guard break-tested: removing the counter hop fails H.02/H.03; restoring the label bug fails G.02/G.04/G.05/G.07/G.15; dropping the already-beaten skip fails I.15/I.16/I.23; letting `resume()` through fails I.10/I.11; reverting the canonicalisation fails F.05/I.21. **F.08 was rewritten after being found to pass for the WRONG REASON** — the old bug ended the script early, which satisfied "finished in a named state" while almost none of it had run; and a first attempt at its replacement comment claimed it would have caught that bug, which break-testing disproved (this fixture's flag is unset, so the branch falls through and the label is never read). The comment now says what is true.

**Not done**: the loss path is unit-tested but not live-driven, and whiteout is not modelled (needs a respawn point — M27I/M27K), so losing leaves the trainer beatable again. `data/map_scripts.json` was regenerated, adding a ~1,670-argument diff to its already-open 8.8 MB tracked-size question.

**[M27F Stage 3 — applymovement/waitmovement, and NPC wandering migrated onto the same runner] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. Entities walk instead of teleporting, and a script can move two of them at once.**

New `scripts/overworld/movement_runner.gd` is THE ONE entity-motion system, with two consumers deliberately sharing it rather than growing a second path beside the first.

⚠️ **THE WANDERING HALF WAS A REAL VISUAL BUG NOBODY HAD SEEN.** `OverworldEntity.cell` is a setter that snaps `position`, so D3's `move_entity()` moved the sprite in ONE frame. Measured on a real wandering NPC before this stage: **6 moves, every per-frame delta exactly 16.0 px = one whole cell, zero intermediate values.** D3 built the movement LOGIC correctly and the visual half was simply never built. **No test could have caught it** — every assertion there is on cells, and the cells were right. Measured after: **198 per-frame moves, 0.02–8.04 px, ZERO whole-cell jumps.**

⚠️ **SCRIPTED MOVEMENT IGNORES COLLISION, DELIBERATELY.** `MovementAction_WalkNormalDown_Step0` calls `InitMovementNormal` with no check of any kind (`event_object_movement.c:7346`). Authored movement is authored — routing it through `StepResolver` the way a player step and NPC wandering both do would strand cutscenes on terrain the author meant to walk over. The WANDERING consumer still resolves first; it just hands the already-approved destination to the runner to animate.

⚠️ **THE COMMIT ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING, because the `cell` setter fights any tween.** Assigning `cell` snaps `position` to the destination, so a mover cannot set the cell and then animate from where it used to be. The order is: capture the old pixel → commit the cell (occupancy updates SYNCHRONOUSLY, which is D3's own invariant — two NPCs must never claim one tile in a frame) → read the new pixel the setter just wrote → **rewind** `position` and interpolate it ourselves. A runner that skipped the rewind would teleport and still pass every cell-based check, which is exactly how the original bug survived.

**THE TWO HALVES ARE ASYMMETRIC AND THAT IS THE POINT.** `applymovement` does **NOT** pause — `ScrCmd_applymovement` starts the movement and returns, and that is the only reason two entities can walk at once, which most of the corridor's cutscenes do. `waitmovement` is the half that blocks. Getting this backwards still "works" for a single-mover cutscene and quietly serialises every multi-mover one. A plain `resume()` **is** correct for `WAIT_MOVEMENT` (there is no result to branch on) — the exact opposite of `WAIT_BATTLE`, which refuses one because resuming would skip the win/loss branch.

**`waitmovement 0` means EVERYTHING, not "object 0"** — `LOCALID_NONE` is 0, so there is no object to name. A named target waits only on that mover.

**Movement scripts needed NO pipeline work, which was the session's best surprise.** They are ordinary labels — the compiler indexes every label uniformly, so `Common_Movement_WalkDown` resolves through the exact same table `goto` uses. Verified region-wide: **2,777 `applymovement` call sites across 1,372 distinct movement scripts, ZERO dangling targets.**

**Ordering in `_process` is load-bearing too**: `tick_movement` sits ABOVE the `_vm` early-return so in-flight motion keeps advancing while a script runs (that IS what a cutscene is), while `tick_entities` — which DECIDES on new wandering moves — sits below it and stays frozen.

**Two cheap source-exact additions, made rather than letting scripts stall.** `walk_slow_*` is **32 frames, not a fraction of 16**: `UpdateWalkSlowAnim` moves one pixel only on EVEN frames (`if (!(sprite->sTimer & 1))`) and ends after 16 such steps, so a slow walk costs exactly double a normal one. And `lock_facing_direction`/`unlock_facing_direction` are a real one-bit flag (`facingDirectionLocked`) — **a locked walk still MOVES, it just does not turn** — modelled properly rather than skipped, because skipping would silently turn a sprite a cutscene meant to hold still. Together they took region-wide coverage from **96.1% → 97.8%** of action occurrences and **89.1% → 92.6%** of movement scripts running to completion (1270/1372). The remaining tail is jumps, slides, affine anims and map-specific macros (`walk_to_gym`, `cut_tree`), none of which the corridor's own cutscenes need.

**A real gap found while writing the tests**: an unimplemented action in the FIRST slot never reaches `tick`, so it was being dropped **silently** — reporting as "a cutscene that did nothing" rather than as a named coverage gap. `start()` now records it the same way `tick` does.

**Frame counts are wall-clock, not `process_frame`** — `[M26G4]` measured frame-tied stepping running ~10% slow at 144 Hz and half speed at 30 Hz, and every discrete stepper in this project has had to learn that once.

**Live-driven, because that is the only thing that would have caught the original bug.** Wandering measured above; scripted movement verified end to end: the player walked (12,0)→(12,2) **interpolated**, `waitmovement` genuinely blocked and released only after 79 process frames (two 16-frame walks of wall clock, not 1–2 frames), and a two-target script moved an NPC (4,8)→(3,8) and the player (12,2)→(11,2) **concurrently**, with occupancy still true at the NPC's new cell afterwards. Disposable driver deleted.

**Tests**: `m27f_script_vm_test` 88 → **111/111**, new Section J (23 assertions) across the action table against source's own step tables, both VM halves and their asymmetry, the runner's commit-then-rewind ordering, mid-flight interpolation, `step_end` termination, unknown-action reporting, instantaneous visibility, the facing lock's both halves, per-mover concurrency, and a region-wide corpus sweep (zero dangling targets; a 1270-script floor as a regression guard so the action table cannot silently shrink). **Three headline guards break-tested and confirmed to catch their regressions**: removing the position rewind fails J.13; making `applymovement` pause fails J.07/J.08/J.11; ignoring the facing lock fails J.18.

**A real, expected regression fixed rather than papered over**: `C.04`/`C.05`/`C.06` used `applymovement` as their "out-of-stage opcode" example — Stage 3 implemented it. Repointed to `checkitemspace`, which is the opcode Brock's own post-battle chain genuinely stops on today (the bag is M27I), so it stays a live example rather than a hypothetical.

**DISCLOSED, pre-existing, not this stage's to invent around: the player sprite does not change facing frame.** `_build_player_node` draws one fixed SOUTH frame and nothing has ever repointed it, so scripted movement updates `_facing` (interaction reads it) without building a second facing implementation alongside `NPC.set_facing`. Worth closing whenever the player-character system lands (M27K).

**Still latent and still unreachable**: an entity crossing a chunk seam mid-movement would have its occupancy written into the wrong chunk's set — `move_entity` keys on the entity's own map. Measured at D3: zero corridor NPCs are both unconstrained on an axis and within one cell of their map edge. A scripted movement could now in principle reach it; none in the corridor does.

Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` **514/514**, `check_bake_diff --all` **32/32 reproducible**.

**[M27F Stage 3b — the walk cycle] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. Entities move their feet instead of sliding.**

Stage 3 made entities MOVE; this makes them WALK. **The gap between those two was invisible to every Stage 3 assertion**, because all of them are on cells and pixels and none on which FRAME is showing — so an NPC glided smoothly to the right tile, turned the right way, and never took a step.

**The data was fully pulled and entirely unused.** `[M27D D1]` pulled 449 sheets; of the 385 resolved ids **136 carry 9 frames and 34 carry 10**, and only 70 are the 3-frame facing-only kind. `FACE_FRAME` only ever indexed 0/1/2, so frames 3-8 on every nine-frame sheet had been imported and never displayed.

**The layout, from `sAnim_Go*` (`src/data/object_events/object_event_anims.h`):** SOUTH steps on 3/4, NORTH on 5/6, WEST on 7/8, and EAST reuses WEST's pair mirrored exactly as its idle frame already does.

⚠️ **THE CYCLE IS FOUR ENTRIES, NOT TWO: `FRAME(stepA) FRAME(idle) FRAME(stepB) FRAME(idle)`.** The resting frame is PART of the walk. Alternating the two step frames alone reads as a shuffle and would pass any "does the frame change" check.

⚠️ **THE CLOCK MUST FREE-RUN ACROSS STEPS, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE MECHANISM.** Source starts a walk with `StartSpriteAnimIfDifferent` — restarting only when the requested anim CHANGES — so three tiles south run one continuous cycle and the feet genuinely alternate. **Restarting per step is the obvious implementation and is wrong in a specific, recognisable way: the same foot leads every tile and the walk reads as a hop.** New `scripts/overworld/walk_anim.gd` keys on facing+speed for exactly this; same key accumulates, different key resets.

**That forced a real exception in the runner, found by writing it wrong first.** `face` means "turn and stand", so it parks the cycle — calling it at the head of every walk (which is what the runner did) restarts the cycle each tile. So a walking action does NOT call `face` **when an `anim` callable is wired**. Source draws the same line: a walk issues `StartSpriteAnimIfDifferent(GO_*)` and never touches the `FACE_*` anim.

⚠️ **THE FIRST CUT OF THAT EXCEPTION WAS AN API TRAP, AND J.18 CAUGHT IT.** Suppressing `face` unconditionally would silently stop turning for any caller supplying only `face` — the pre-3b contract, and every test that stubs one callable. **This is not a stale-assumption case; the test was right and the change was wrong.** The suppression is now conditional on an animation actually being wired, so old callers are untouched.

**A locked facing blocks the TURN, not the walk** — `facingDirectionLocked` leaves the current GO anim running — so the cycle holds its established direction and keeps animating rather than freezing.

**Walking in place ANIMATES despite `moves == false`**, which is the entire point of it; source routes it through the same GO anims. Gating the animation on `moves` would leave every "look busy" cutscene beat standing perfectly still.

**Slow is the asymmetry worth knowing: there is NO `GoSlow*` anim in source** (the standard table is FACE / GO / GO_FAST / GO_FASTER / GO_FASTEST only), so a slow walk reuses the NORMAL 8-tick entry and its 32 movement frames play **four** cycle entries rather than two stretched ones. A slow walk shows more foot movement per tile, not slower foot movement.

**THE PLAYER IS INCLUDED, WHICH IS A DELIBERATE SMALL WIDENING OF THE ASK.** Rob asked for NPC walking animations; NPCs animating while the player slid facing south forever would read as a bug within one playtest, and the player draws from the same sheets by the same rules. **This closes Stage 3's own disclosed gap** — that comment ("the player sprite does not change facing frame") is retired. ⚠️ **The player has TWO movement paths and both had to be wired**: the ordinary input step is a plain Tween, not a runner script. Resting is gated on the direction being RELEASED rather than on the tween finishing, because between two steps of a held walk there is one frame where `_moving` is already false and the next `_try_step` has not run — resting there would reset the cycle every tile and reintroduce the hop.

**The player's cadence is DERIVED, not copied.** Source pairs a 16-frame walk with 8-tick entries — two entries per tile. This project's player step is 0.16s (~9.6 frames), its own tuning choice, so the literal 8 would run the feet at source's rate under a faster body and read as skating. Preserving the INVARIANT (two entries per tile) rather than the CONSTANT keeps the cadence matched to whatever the duration is tuned to later.

**Sheets that cannot animate still have to face correctly** — 70 ids carry three frames and 96 carry one — so a sub-9-frame sheet draws its idle frame and never indexes past the end of its strip.

**Generated, not hand-edited**: the frame tables live in `gen_object_event_sprites.py` and were regenerated; the 385-row id table came back byte-identical, so only constants changed.

**Live-driven, because that is what caught Stage 3's own bug and what tests here are structurally blind to.** Real scene, real input, sampling the frame index actually showing: the player walking south produced **`[0, 3, 0, 4, 0, 3, 0, 4, 0, 3, 0]`** — the exact source cycle, alternating across tiles rather than restarting; north ran its own pair (`5`/`6`) and east reused west's (`7`/`2`); a wandering NPC showed the correct pair for each direction segment across 192 moving frames. Disposable driver deleted.

**Tests**: `m27f_script_vm_test` 111 → **132/132**, new Section K (21 assertions) across the frame tables against source, the four-entry cycle and its loop, the free-running clock in both directions, idempotent resting, sheets that cannot animate, which ACTIONS animate, the slow-walk four-entry asymmetry, and the runner's own driving and settling. **Four headline guards break-tested**: restarting the cycle per step fails K.08; alternating stepA/stepB only fails K.04/K.05/K.08; dropping the settle-to-idle fails K.18; gating the animation on `moves` fails K.15b.

⚠️ **K.15 WAS FOUND VACUOUS BY ITS OWN INJECTION AND IS THE LESSON HERE.** It asserted the action TABLE carries an anim tick for walk-in-place — true, and green even with the runner's own gate broken, because it never drove the runner. K.15b now does, and is what actually fails. A guard whose injection you had to hand-pick is a guard you have not tested.

Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` **514/514**, `check_bake_diff --all` **32/32 reproducible** (the new NPC field is not exported, so no baked scene changed).

**[M27F Stage 3c — the trainer approach, migrated onto the runner] COMPLETE — 2026-07-31. The last teleporter is gone.**

Rob, after Stage 3b: *"research why trainers on approach are still not animated."* **Because the approach never went through the MovementRunner.** `_run_trainer_approach` stepped with a bare `manager.move_entity(...)` on a 0.18s timer — D3's raw commit, which assigns `cell` and lets the setter snap `position` — so an approaching trainer jumped a whole tile per tick with no interpolation and, after Stage 3b, no walk cycle either.

⚠️ **STAGE 3'S OWN RECORD SAID "TWO CONSUMERS". THERE WERE THREE.** Wandering and scripted `applymovement` were both migrated; the approach was missed because **D4 built it before the runner existed** and nothing pointed the two at each other. Enumerated now, so the next one cannot hide: the only remaining direct `move_entity` callers are the runner's own commit closure (correct by construction) and one occupancy test (deliberate).

**The migration also made the pacing MORE accurate, which was not the goal.** `APPROACH_STEP_SECONDS = 0.18` was an invented feel value; source hands the approaching object `GetWalkNormalMovementAction` (`trainer_see.c`) — a NORMAL walk, 16 frames a tile. Routing through the runner therefore replaces a guess with source's real cadence, and the constant is **retired** rather than left dangling.

**New shared `MapManager.walk_ops(dir, count)`** builds the script for both callers, so wandering and the approach cannot drift into two spellings of the same thing. ⚠️ **`count` is `distance - 1`** — the adjacent-stop off-by-one D4 ported from `CheckTrainer` — and a trainer already adjacent must yield a script that ends immediately rather than one stray step onto the player.

**`_await_movement` is bounded, not open-ended.** A runner that never clears — an unimplemented action, a freed node — would otherwise hang the approach forever *with input still locked*, which is unrecoverable for the player. The budget is twice the walk's own wall-clock cost plus a second, so it can only fire on a genuine fault.

**Occupancy is unchanged**: the runner commits each cell at its action's START, so D3's "two entities cannot claim one tile in a frame" invariant holds exactly as it did with the direct call.

**Live-driven.** A real approach on Route 3 (Lass Robin, sight 3): **115 per-frame moves, 0.05–0.43 px, ZERO whole-cell jumps**, the walk cycle running throughout, ending adjacent to the player. Before this change the same approach was two moves of exactly 16.0 px.

⚠️ **THE PROBE RAISED A QUESTION IT COULD NOT ANSWER, AND THE ANSWER BECAME A TEST.** Its frame trace showed an extra step-pair, which is the signature of a cycle restart. Chasing it through the probe was a dead end — that trainer also WANDERS, so most of the trace was wandering, not approach. The real question — *does the cycle survive the action boundary inside one multi-tile script?* — is now pinned deterministically by **K.24** instead of inferred from a noisy trace. It does; a restart there would make a three-tile approach hop toward you.

**Tests**: `m27f_script_vm_test` 132 → **136/136** (K.21–K.24: the shared builder, the adjacent-trainer zero-step case, and the action-boundary continuity). K.24 break-tested — restarting the cycle per action fails it and K.08 together.

Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` **514/514**, `check_bake_diff --all` **32/32 reproducible**.

**[M27F — configurable start point] 2026-07-31. Play now begins in Pewter City, by the Route 3 gate.**

Rob: *"change my starting location to Pewter City so I don't have to walk so far to get to trainers."* Set on the SCENE (`overworld.tscn`), not as the script default — the code default stays `PalletTown_Frlg`, so anything constructing the controller directly is unchanged.

**Changing the map alone was not enough.** `_spawn_player` used `_first_walkable`, a row-major scan, which in Pewter lands on the north edge at (23, 1) — **52 tiles from the nearest trainer**, which defeats the point. New `start_cell` export spawns at (45, 21) beside the Route 3 gate instead: **20 tiles**, with Route 2 and Route 3 both loading as neighbours and all 8 of Route 3's trainers reachable without a warp.

⚠️ **`start_cell` is VALIDATED, not trusted, and the failure it prevents is nasty.** An unwalkable cell would drop the player inside scenery, where the step resolver refuses every direction and the only escape is a warp they cannot reach — unrecoverable, and silent. It falls back to `_first_walkable` and warns by name. Break-tested against a genuinely solid tile: warns, falls back, still spawns walkable.

⚠️ **FOUR TESTS DEPENDED ON THE START MAP IMPLICITLY** — they instantiated `overworld.tscn` and asserted Pallet-specific things without ever setting `start_map`, so a play-convenience change would have broken them. Now explicit. This is the same fixture fragility `[M27C]` already paid for once, when a "known unbaked" map got baked and took ten assertions down with it: **a fixture that depends on a default someone may reasonably change is a fixture that will break.**

Regression: `m27a_step_resolver_test` **514/514**, `m27f_script_vm_test` **136/136**, `check_bake_diff --all` **32/32**.

**[M27F — trainer-battle family + small opcode batch] 2026-08-06. 121 distinct commands of source's 237, up from 90.**

A recon pass (report-only, no code) first mapped M27F's own remaining gaps precisely — distinct from M27G's separately-scoped `special`/`specialvar` surface, which this session does not touch. Every genuinely open item traced to a real citation in `script_vm.gd`'s own dispatch (`Pause.UNKNOWN_OP` + `diagnostic`, the mechanism that IS the gap inventory — there is no separate "known missing opcodes" checklist by design). Then a region-wide corpus scan (`data/map_scripts.json`, cross-referenced against the real `asm/macros/event.inc` field-macro list — 393 real field opcodes, filtered out of the 1,093 raw op names the file also carries, since the same indexer sweeps in battle-animation and contest-AI scripts) ranked every still-unimplemented field opcode by real usage, to separate genuine quick wins from cosmetic completeness.

**The trainer-battle family was the headline finding.** `[M27F Stage 2]` shipped exactly one of source's five real dispatch shapes (`trainerbattle_single`) — the other four (`trainerbattle_no_intro`/`_double`/`_rematch`/`_rematch_double`) halted with `UNKNOWN_OP` every time, a combined **496 corpus uses**. `_trainer_battle` was extracted into a shared `_start_trainer_battle(trainer, intro, defeat, continuation, skip_already_beaten_check)`, reused by all five variants with zero behavior change to the already-tested `trainerbattle_single` path (confirmed: `m27f_script_vm_test`'s pre-existing 136 assertions passed unchanged before any new test was added).

⚠️ **`trainerbattle_rematch`'s already-beaten check is DELIBERATELY BYPASSED, and the reason is a real gap, not a convenience.** Source's own dispatch (`BattleSetup_GetTrainerBattleScript`, `battle_setup.c:1144`) remaps the trainer id through `GetRematchTrainerId` *before* the already-beaten check is ever reached — a rematch-tier lookup table this project does not have (M35's full rematch/postgame progression, still deferred). Without a stronger roster to remap to, the disclosed simplification is to re-fight the SAME trainer's existing static roster, which only works if this opcode's own internal beaten-check does not itself refuse the fight. Safe because the CALLING script is what decides whether a rematch is even offered (the standard shape upstream of a rematch trigger is a `goto_if_defeated` already gating it) — this opcode does not need to re-derive that. `trainerbattle_double`'s own `not_enough_pkmn_text` argument is parsed and explicitly discarded, not wired: `OverworldParty.build_trainer_party` already flattens every doubles trainer to a singles fight (`[M27D D5]`'s own disclosed "the overworld has no two-active concept yet" gap) — dispatching a double battle identically to a single one is honest about what actually happens, not a new gap this session introduced.

**Six more opcodes closed alongside it**, each independently source-verified against `scrcmd.c`: `checkplayergender` (reads the real `PlayerIdentity.gender` via `TextBuffers.active_identity()`, matching `gSaveBlock2Ptr->playerGender`'s own 0/1 encoding exactly — no remapping needed since M27K K-b already built real gender selection); `random` (`Random() % max`, a defensive `limit<=0` guard added since C's own `%0` is undefined behavior no real script triggers); `setorcopyvar` (source keeps a real raw-storage-vs-`VarGet` distinction between this and `copyvar`, but this project's own `FlagStore.var_get` is already the one accessor every var read goes through regardless of opcode, so the two are indistinguishable here — disclosed rather than silently assumed identical); `waitse`/`playmoncry`/`waitmoncry` (the same audio-does-not-exist no-op class as the pre-existing `playfanfare` group); `bufferboxname` (a real HALT, not a no-op — mirrors `_begin_nickname`'s own PC-branch precedent exactly: there is no PC (I5-5, still deferred), so there is no real box name to buffer, and inventing one would be the same "near-miss that reads as working" this project's PC-nickname gap already refuses to risk); `fadescreenspeed`/`fadescreenswapbuffers` (the two `fadescreen` siblings named-but-excluded when that opcode shipped, same no-op reasoning now extended to both).

**A symbolic-constant table closes `addvar`/`subvar`/`checkmoney`/`addmoney`/`removemoney`'s own long-disclosed gap** — 11 real constants (`ROULETTE_SPECIAL_RATE=1<<7`, 7 flute/furniture prices, `MAGIKARP_PRICE`, `COINS_PRICE_50`/`_500`), each individually confirmed via direct source citation (`.set`/`.equ` in the owning map script, or a `#define` in `include/constants/roulette.h`). ⚠️ **Every one of them is confirmed Hoenn-only content** (Route 113's Glass Workshop, Mauville's Game Corner, the Route 4 Pokémon Center) — not merely outside the current corridor, content this Kanto project will never author a map for at all. Kept anyway since the values are cheap and sourced, and "resolves to a real number" is strictly safer than the fail-closed 0 it replaces.

⚠️ **A real, expected regression was found and fixed in `m27i_wallet_test.gd`'s own pre-existing C.05/C.05b/C.05c.** Those three assertions had `COINS_PRICE_500` as their own worked example of "an unresolvable price fails closed" — exactly the gap this session just closed, so they started failing the moment the fix landed. The same "a genuine correctness fix legitimately invalidates a stale test assumption" shape this project has hit before (Nature/Solar-Beam/ban-flag regressions, etc.): rewritten to confirm `COINS_PRICE_500` now resolves to its real value (10000) and the wallet actually charges it, with a NEW C.05d/e/f added on a deliberately synthetic constant name (`SOME_UNRESOLVED_PRICE_CONSTANT`) to keep the fail-closed path covered without the guard being invalidated again the next time a real constant is added to the table. `m27i_wallet_test` grew 30→33 (34/34 incl. its own Z.99).

New assertions added directly to `m27f_script_vm_test.gd` as Sections L (trainer-battle family, 16 assertions incl. the key already-beaten-bypass discriminator and a same-trainer `trainerbattle_single` negative control proving it's real) and M (the small opcode batch + symbolic constants, 14 assertions incl. a 200-sample statistical range check for `random` and a genuinely-unlisted-constant regression guard proving the table is additive, not a blanket resolve-everything change) — `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 135→165, **166/166** including Z.99.

No rerunnable corpus-coverage tool was built either — the last recorded overall percentage (27.5%, Stage 2 era) stays stale; only the region-wide movement-script floor (`m27f_script_vm_test`'s own J.22/23, 1270/1372) is still tracked live.

**[M27G recon] 2026-08-06. Scope of record is `docs/m27g_recon.md` — this is a summary, not a replacement. 594 region-wide functions correct to 51 reachable, then to ~20-25 real ones.**

Every prior citation of this milestone's size ("1,390 `special` + 719 `specialvar` = 2,109 uses across 569 distinct functions") measured the WHOLE region-wide compiled corpus — Kanto and Hoenn mixed indiscriminately, the same blind spot `[M27F]`'s own opcode-frequency scan already corrected once for plain opcodes, now applied to a much larger nominal surface where it matters far more. A reachability BFS from the 32 actually-baked corridor maps' own real entry points (193 placed-entity `script_label`s + each map's own `OnTransition`/`OnFrame`/`OnWarp`/`OnLoad`/`OnResume` table, walked through `call`/`goto`/every conditional's jump target) finds only **51 distinct functions, 99 total uses, reachable from content that exists in this project today.** `callnative` never fires from the corridor at all.

**A second cut removed multiplayer-gated call sites even where graph-reachable** — traced by CALLER, not just presence: `CloseLink` (13 uses) is exclusively `CableClub_EventScript_Abort*` handlers; `SavePlayerParty`/`LoadPlayerBag` only fire from the Battle Colosseum's own Cable Club entry; `HasEnoughMonsForDoubleBattle`/`HasAtLeastOneBerry` only from Cable-Club double-battle-mode/Wireless-Berry-Crush selectors. All already covered by this project's own standing multiplayer/Union-Room/Easy-Chat exclusion. **Real actionable scope: ~20-25 distinct functions, ~30-40 uses** — the number worth building against, not 594 and not even 51.

**Headline finding: the Running Shoes NPC (`PewterCity_EventScript_AideGiveRunningShoes`) is real, in-corridor content whose own script tail is `setflag FLAG_SYS_B_DASH`** — the first genuine in-game trigger for Run, which `[M27E E2]`'s own status note already flagged as granted ONLY by the debug boot ("no in-game event grants `FLAG_SYS_B_DASH` yet"). The only thing blocking this script is `DisableMsgBoxWalkaway` plus two VM opcodes this recon found along the way (`signmsg`/`normalmsg`) — all three confirmed, by direct source citation, to be LITERAL NO-OPS already in the reference itself (`script_cmd_table.inc`'s own dispatch sends both opcodes to `ScrCmd_nop1`; `DisableMsgBoxWalkaway`/`SetWalkingIntoSignVars`'s own C bodies are single commented-out lines in source, `script.c:685-692`) — not a simplification, a faithful port of an already-inert function.

**Tier 0 (trivial, source-confirmed no-op or already-built bridge, no open design question)**: the 3 opcodes/specials above, `HealPlayerParty`/`ChangePokemonNickname` (already fully implemented — the latter dispatched specially inside `ScriptVM.step()` itself, not through `FieldSpecials`, so its absence from that registry isn't a gap), `OpenMuseumFossilPic`/`CloseMuseumFossilPic` (matches the already-established `showmonpic`/`hidemonpic` field-picture-layer no-op precedent, reachable from Pewter Museum's real Kabutops/Aerodactyl fossil NPCs), `SaveGame` (a bridge to the already-built M27L save system), `CountPartyNonEggMons`/`CalculatePlayerPartyCount`/`ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` (cheap reads off `OverworldSession.player_party()`, the same shape `getpartysize` already established).

**Tier 2 (real functions, already-scoped elsewhere)**: the Pokédex family → M33; `ChooseMonForMoveTutor` → M30; `DoPCTurnOnEffect`/`BedroomPC` → M27I I5-5 (deferred); `ShouldTryRematchBattle` (311 uses region-wide, the single highest-usage function found in the ENTIRE scan, 0 reachable from this corridor) → M35; `IsBadEggInParty` → M31; `GetLeadMonFriendship` → **no milestone owns a friendship system yet**, flagged not assigned.

**Tier 3 (permanently excluded, confirmed by real call-site tracing, not just presence)**: 19 functions, all Cable Club/Union Room/Easy Chat/Battle Frontier — full list in the recon doc.

**Deliberately not attempted**: scoping the remaining ~540 region-wide functions this corridor can't reach — dominated by Contest Hall/Secret Bases/the 7 Battle Frontier facilities/Trainer Hill/Trainer Tower/Pokéblocks/the Devon Corporation plotline, content that's either already excluded or unjudgeable before its own maps are ever baked. **Recommendation recorded in the doc: re-run this exact reachability walk every time a new batch of maps is baked, rather than pre-scoping the whole region now** — the same "vertical slice, not full-region import" discipline `[M27B]` already established for the map-baking side of this exact problem.

**`ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies`/`BufferMonNickname` needed real party context, so — matching the existing `ChangePokemonNickname` precedent — both are dispatched directly inside `ScriptVM.step()` rather than through `FieldSpecials`, which is deliberately stateless by its own design.** `ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` (source: `field_specials.c:1641-1644`, `GetMonData(&gParties[B_TRAINER_PLAYER][gSpecialVar_0x8004], MON_DATA_SPECIES_OR_EGG, NULL)`) is party-ONLY with no PC fallback in source itself, unlike the one below — new `_party_mon_species(slot)`, degrading to 0 (no species) on an out-of-range slot, the same "unknown constant" shape `_literal` already uses. `BufferMonNickname` (source: `tv.c:3273-3278`, via `GetSelectedBoxMonFromPcOrParty`) DOES support a PC path in source — guarded with the exact same `PC_MON_CHOSEN` halt `_begin_nickname()`'s own PC branch already established, rather than silently assuming party-only. Both found via `PalletTown_RivalsHouse_EventScript_GroomMon` (Daisy's grooming) — real, reachable, non-excluded content; confirmed via its own full script that `BufferMonNickname` is needed at its tail regardless of `DaisyMassageServices`'s own still-blocked friendship effect, so it was folded into G1 rather than left for later.

**[M27G G2] 2026-08-06. `ChoosePartyMon`, bridging to the already-built `FieldPartyScreen` in browse mode — and a real, general `PARTY_SIZE` gap found only by driving the real corpus.**

`special ChoosePartyMon` is a new pause kind, `Pause.WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE`, matching `WAIT_BATTLE`/`WAIT_NAMING`'s own shape exactly: it carries a RESULT (the chosen slot), so a plain `resume()` deliberately cannot clear it — `resume()`'s own guard extended, `is_waiting()` extended — and only a new `answer_party_choice(index: int)` can. `index` is whatever the real screen's own `confirm()`/`close()` already report (a 0-5 slot, or -1 on cancel); both -1 AND an out-of-range index write `PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN` (0xFF, `include/constants/party_menu.h:5`) into `VAR_0x8004`, matching source's own `>= PARTY_SIZE` check (`BufferMonSelection`, `party_menu.c:8046-8052`) rather than trusting the screen's own -1 convention is the only "nothing chosen" shape a caller could hand in. Dispatched inside `ScriptVM.step()` itself (not `FieldSpecials`, which stays deliberately stateless), the same precedent `ChangePokemonNickname`/`BufferMonNickname` already set.

`overworld.gd` gained a new `_vm_party_choice_pending: bool`, checked FIRST in both `_on_party_mon_chosen`/`_on_party_cancelled` — a third disambiguation alongside `_pending_use_item`'s own two states (item-use target-picker vs. a plain browse) — routing to `answer_party_choice(index)`/`answer_party_choice(-1)` respectively before either handler falls through to its existing item-use logic. The driver's own `match _vm.pause_reason:` block opens `_party_screen.open(_vm.party)` in plain browse mode (no item name), guarded on `_vm_party_choice_pending` rather than `_party_screen.is_open` so a re-entrant frame — the screen takes a frame to actually open — cannot double-open it.

⚠️ **DRIVING THE REAL CORPUS FOUND A GENERAL, NOT HOENN-ONLY, GAP: `PARTY_SIZE` WAS UNRESOLVED.** `PalletTown_RivalsHouse_EventScript_GroomMon`'s own very next opcode after `ChoosePartyMon` is `goto_if_ge VAR_0x8004, PARTY_SIZE, DeclineGrooming` — the real "was anything chosen" check — and `_literal` had no case for `PARTY_SIZE` at all, falling through to its generic unknown-constant 0. Since `VAR_0x8004` is always `>= 0`, this made the check unconditionally true: **every one of the 57 region-wide corpus uses of `PARTY_SIZE`** (nearly all in exactly this `goto_if_ge VAR_0x8004, PARTY_SIZE, Decline*` shape — trade requests, minigame entry, grooming) always took the Decline/abort branch regardless of what was actually picked. Fixed with one new `_literal` case, `"PARTY_SIZE": return BattleParty.PARTY_SIZE` (`include/constants/global.h:82` = 6) — found only because G2's own test drove the real compiled script rather than a synthetic fixture, the same discipline that caught G1's `signmsg`/`normalmsg` gap.

**Daisy's grooming script now runs past `ChoosePartyMon` all the way to its own real remaining blocker.** Live-driven (`data/map_scripts.json`/`data/map_texts.json`): the offer's own yes/no → `WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE` → answering with a real slot → `ScriptGetPartyMonSpecies` genuinely reads that slot's real species (proving the `PARTY_SIZE` fix, not just asserting it — a still-broken build would read `VAR_RESULT == 0`, never having reached the `specialvar` at all) → the no-op `fadescreen`/`playfanfare`/`waitfanfare` chain → halts cleanly at `special DaisyMassageServices`, which needs a friendship system this project doesn't have (unowned, flagged in the recon doc's own Tier 2) — not a halt anywhere earlier than that.

New assertions added directly to `m27f_script_vm_test.gd` as Section O (11 assertions): the VM contract in isolation (`WAIT_PARTY_CHOICE` set, `is_waiting()`, a plain `resume()` NOT clearing it, a valid choice writing the real slot and letting the script continue to `DONE`, a cancel and an out-of-range index both degrading to `PARTY_NOTHING_CHOSEN`, and the pause being a genuine no-op once already resolved); then the real corpus test driving Daisy's script end to end, including the `PARTY_SIZE` discriminator. `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 174→185, **186/186 including Z.99**, all green after two real test-authoring fixes (an `_run`-vs-`_drive` mixup that can't auto-resume a `message` wait; the script's own cooldown-counter gate needing `VAR_MASSAGE_COOLDOWN_STEP_COUNTER` primed above 500 or it takes the unimplemented `GetLeadMonFriendship` branch first) — both test bugs, not production bugs.

Regression: `m27f_script_vm_test` **186/186**, plus all 19 other non-hanging overworld suites reconfirmed clean via `run_overworld_tests.sh` (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 514/514 down through `m27o_whiteout_test` 33/33, no engine ERROR lines). `m27h_catching_test.tscn` skipped, the same already-documented pre-existing hang. Full battle-side sweep not run this session — nothing outside `scripts/overworld/`/`scenes/overworld/` was touched, and full sweeps are Rob's own manual step per this file's standing convention.

**[M27G G3 recon] 2026-08-06. Report-only Step 0 into `trade.c`, per its own build-order note — full detail in `docs/m27g_recon.md`'s new "G3 Step 0" section. Small and bounded, not the open-ended item the old note implied.**

The four functions (`GetTradeSpecies`/`GetInGameTradeSpeciesInfo`/`CreateInGameTradePokemon`/`DoInGameTradeScene`, all `trade.c`) sit behind four shared `EventScript_*` helper scripts (`data/event_scripts.s:1531-1551`) riding entirely on already-shipped opcode infrastructure (`special`/`specialvar`/`goto_if_*` — no new opcode class, the same shape G1/G2's own additions were). ⚠️ **`DoInGameTradeScene` is NOT a lightweight fade-and-swap — it invokes the full link-trade cinematic engine** (`CB2_InitInGameTrade`/`CB2_InGameTrade`, ball-bounce + GBA screen zoom + cable sprites), the identical spectacle a real link trade runs, just with `isLinkTrade = FALSE`; confirmed via direct trace to be genuinely un-multiplayer-gated on this path (never touches `HandleLinkDataSend`/`SendBlock`), so the standing Cable-Club exclusion does not apply.

**One real trade NPC is already reachable today**: `Route2_House_EventScript_Reyley` (Mr. Mime ↔ Abra) is fully compiled into `data/map_scripts.json`/`map_texts.json` on `Route2_House_Frlg` — an already-baked map — all 4 branches (decline/wrong-species/already-traded/success) present and waiting only on the 4 specials. The other 7 real Kanto trade NPCs (Route 11, Underground Path, Cerulean, Vermilion, Cinnabar Lab ×2, Route 18) sit on unbaked maps — a map-baking question, not a G3 one; they fall out for free once baked, since the specials are generic across the table.

**Reuse confirmed for almost everything**: `ChoosePartyMon` (G2) is the exact selection screen needed; `PokemonFactory.create_battle_pokemon`'s existing `forced_ivs`/`ability_slot` params (built ahead of need, per that function's own doc comment) cover the incoming mon's construction; `nickname`/`held_item` are already real settable `BattlePokemon` fields; the M26B3 ball recall/send-out animation primitives are a real precedent for a future visual beat. **Genuinely new, but small**: an 8-entry trades data table (0 of 13 region-wide entries are FRLG-relevant beyond 8 — the first 4 are RSE-only), the 4 specials themselves dispatched inside `ScriptVM.step()` (matching the `ChoosePartyMon`/`BufferMonNickname` precedent, since `FieldSpecials` stays stateless), and — ⚠️ **a SECOND `_literal` symbolic-constant gap, the identical shape as G2's own `PARTY_SIZE` find**: `INGAME_TRADE_MR_MIME` etc. (`include/constants/trade.h:12-25`) has no case in `_literal` and would silently resolve to 0, sending every trade NPC's own `setvar VAR_0x8008, INGAME_TRADE_*` to the wrong table row — flagged now so it's fixed alongside implementation rather than found later via a silent wrong-branch bug. The one real new mechanism (not just a new dispatch case) is the party swap itself — remove the offered mon, insert the constructed traded-in mon at the same slot — since nothing in `BattleParty`/`OverworldSession` currently does that specific splice.
