**Reachability**: no in-game event grants `FLAG_SYS_B_DASH` yet, so the debug boot grants it (pinned by F.05, or running would be dead code in play).

**Tests**: new `m27e_run_test` **45/45**. Four injections, each failing exactly what it should and nothing else: reverting the player sprite to `LEAF` fails E.01/E.07/E.08/E.09; an even 4,4,4,4 cadence fails B.04 **and D.06** (the sample that sits inside entry 0 only because it is 5 units long — D.01-D.05 pass under both); a gate ignoring the held key fails E.02; dropping long grass fails C.02. Z.99 caught two miscounts of my own before either could ship. Regression: **all 21 overworld suites green**, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32 reproducible, and the sprite regeneration left **only the 3 intended sheets** changed (berry trees restored byte-for-byte once the geometry guard landed).

⚠️ **DISCLOSED — NOT LIVE-DRIVEN.** The cycle maths, the gate, the cadence and the composited sheet are all test- and pixel-verified, but **nobody has held Shift and watched it**. The two things most likely to read wrong on screen are the ones a test cannot see: whether 0.08s/tile actually feels like running rather than skating, and whether the 5:3 gait reads correctly at that speed. This project's own record is unambiguous that driving finds what suites cannot.

⚠️ **ONE DELIBERATE CONSEQUENCE, FLAGGED RATHER THAN LEFT TO SURPRISE SOMEONE**: `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_OLD_MAN_2` is now composited too (9 -> 10 frames), which is source-faithful — its table references only frames 0,1,2 plus one OldWoman frame, so that NPC becomes effectively facing-only where it previously used the raw sheet's real step frames. **One consumer, one map** (`PewterCity_Museum_2F_Frlg`), and unwatched.

⚠️ **[M27E E2 — SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY: THE COMPOSITE IS GONE.]** Everything above shipped in commit `a7fcf13a` and was then **reverted at Rob's direction**, 2026-08-04. Kept rather than rewritten (this file does not edit history), but **do not act on the composite half of it** — the generator no longer composites anything, and `gen_object_event_sprites.py`'s multi-symbol handling, `_write_composite`, the geometry guard and the `mixed geom` report are all removed.

**Rob's objection, and it was right**: *"Seems like it shouldn't be [a composite] with the run sheet available."* The run frames are already a usable sheet on disk — `leaf/green_surf_run.png`, raw frames 3-13, sitting beside the three surf poses at 0-2. Materialising a stitched 20-frame copy of a file we already ship is manufacturing an asset to justify machinery.

**What replaced it**: the run frames are read from that sheet directly and `WalkAnim` **swaps the sprite's texture for the duration of a run**. New `setup_run(run_graphics_id)` binds the run sheet; `_draw` takes a `run` flag and swaps only when the cycle actually crosses between sheets; `setup()` caches the walk texture and clears the applied-sheet marker, so the surf path (which assigns `sprite.texture` directly) can never leave this class believing a stale sheet is mounted.

**Consequences of the switch, all in the good direction:**
  * **zero asset churn** — all three sheets are byte-identical to their pre-composite state again, verified against `b5ada413`;
  * **the OldMan2 side effect is gone** — that NPC is back to its own 9-frame sheet, so the flagged one-map behaviour change no longer exists;
  * **the berry-tree hazard is moot** — nothing composites, so nothing can crop a grown stage. ⚠️ The underlying finding still stands and is now *purely* informational: `gen_object_event_sprites.py` still emits only the FIRST file of any multi-symbol pic table, so a berry tree still renders its dirt pile and never its grown stages. **That is a real, open, pre-existing gap this feature no longer touches.**

⚠️ **THE COST IS A -6 INDEX SHIFT, AND IT IS THE ONE PLACE AN ERROR CAN HIDE.** Source's `sAnim_Run*Frlg` quote 9/12/15 because its pic table stitches the two files; reading the file directly makes every index 6 lower (3/6/9, legs 4/5, 7/8, 10/11). Pinned **twice** — arithmetically against source's own numbers (B.06) and by **pixels** (A.05-A.07: no run index may land on a surf pose, and the three neutrals must be distinct real art). `MIN_RAW_FRAMES_TO_RUN = 12` is compared against the **sheet's measured width**, never a graphics id's declared `frames`, because `FRAME_OVERRIDES` deliberately reports 3 for the surf ids and an id-based check would refuse the very sheet the run frames live on.

⚠️ **`PLAYER_GRAPHICS_ID` STAYS `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_NORMAL`, AND UNDER THE SWAP IT MATTERS MORE, NOT LESS.** The run frames are swapped in from Green's sheet, so if the player still walked as `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_LEAF` they would visibly change **character** every time Shift went down. Pinned by E.08.

⚠️ **A REAL MISTAKE MADE AND CAUGHT DURING THE REVERT, worth recording**: reverting the generator to `b5ada413` reverted it **one commit too far** — `FRAME_OVERRIDES` was added by E1c, which was part of `a7fcf13a` rather than before it — silently restoring `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_GREEN_SURF` to 14 declared frames and making the surf sheet animate again. **Caught by `m27e_surf_test` (74 -> 71/74)**, not by inspection; a checkout-based revert reverts everything in that file, including things the session never touched.

**Tests**: `m27e_run_test` 45 -> **46/46**, re-based to raw indices. Three fresh injections: unshifted pic indices fail **8** assertions across A/B/D (bounds, tables and cycle independently); pointing the run sheet at the walk sheet fails E.01/E.07/E.08/E.09; reverting to the cameo sprite fails E.08. Regression: all **21 overworld suites** green, `check_bake_diff --all` 32/32.

**Still NOT live-driven** — unchanged from above, and the swap adds one thing worth watching that a test cannot see: whether the texture change at run start/stop is invisible in motion.

## M27M — Map authoring tooling *(new block, scoped and approved 2026-07-30)*

**[M27M-T — trimmed TileSet] ⚠️ OUT OF SCOPE — Rob's call, 2026-08-09. CUT, not deferred.** Re-measured that day against the post-Part-C tree and the numbers came back BETTER than the original recon (6.1x tile definitions and 10.0x load, from real trimmed twins, against Rev 1's 4.0x/8.9x) — and it was cut anyway, with those numbers in hand. **So a future session finding it worthwhile has found nothing new and must not re-propose it.** Accepted cost: TileSet construction is ~21 ms per pair, so boot grows toward ~1.3 s once all 60 pairs are built. One correction from that pass is worth keeping independently of the tier, because other work can rely on it: ⚠️ **a metatile routes to exactly TWO of the three planes, never three** — the original recon multiplied by 3 and so overstated the work by 50%. Full detail in `docs/m27m_trimmed_tileset_recon.md` §0, kept as a record rather than a plan. Original scoping follows.

**[M27M-T — trimmed TileSet] SCOPED 2026-07-30, not built. ⚠️ Scope of record is `docs/m27m_trimmed_tileset_recon.md`.** Measured rather than estimated: a real trimmed twin of the Pallet Town pair loads in **1.71 ms against the full set's 15.25 ms — 8.9x**, 25 KB → 6 KB. Region-wide only **11,036 tile definitions are actually placed** against the 132,480 `create_tile()` calls made today (**4.0x**), and all fourteen corridor pairs together would build in **~31 ms** — about what ONE cold tileset costs now. Baked scenes need no re-bake (M27M2 made the TileSet an `ext_resource`), `check_bake_diff` and the overlay are unaffected, and no consumer iterates tiles. **The failure mode is SILENT and is the whole design constraint**: `set_cell` accepts a coord whose tile was never created, stores it faithfully, and renders nothing — so the trim pass must ship with its own coverage proof, not after. Three hazards are recorded there with measurements: the trim set must be computed region-wide per pair rather than per-bake (or a later map silently renders with holes), it must union `border[]` as well as `metatile[]` (**5 border ids region-wide appear in no map body**), and it conflicts head-on with M27M's authoring requirement — resolved by treating `trim`/`expand` as two idempotent operations on ONE artifact rather than shipping two files.

**A thirteenth M27 block, orthogonal to C's stitching work.** Added because this is Kanto with an ORIGINAL story: the region's 421 maps are imported, but new maps and new art are inevitable, and **there is currently no way to create either.** Scope of record while this is unbuilt: the approved mockup at `https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0cc55049-a0d4-4675-8b0c-eb33853611b0`. Nothing here is implemented.

**The architectural decision this rests on, Rob's call: THE SCENE BECOMES THE SOURCE OF TRUTH.** Import Kanto once, then hand the artifacts over to authoring rather than keeping a re-runnable pipeline. That retires machinery deliberately rather than by neglect — the baker's `_scene_divergence` guard and `check_bake_diff` both answer *"does this scene still contain only what the baker would produce?"*, and once maps carry hand-painted art **every map is non-reproducible by design**, so both become permanent false positives (the same failure fixed this session, but semantic and unfixable). `provenance` loses its guard role for the same reason. **`attr_explicit` matters MORE, not less** — it marks cells whose collision/elevation is an inherited guess, which is exactly what every newly painted cell is. Keep both guards until the Kanto import is declared done, then retire them on purpose. Accepted cost: a bug found later has no bulk-fix path except a script that rewrites `.tres` files directly.

**Painting already works and needs nothing built** — `_build_tileset` calls `create_tile` for EVERY tile in the atlas, not just used ones, so Godot's own tilemap editor can paint any metatile in a pair today. What is missing is everything downstream of the paint.

**Three findings that shaped the design.** (1) **A `behavior` custom data layer is already declared on every baked TileSet and never populated** — nothing writes it, nothing reads it; someone scaffolded this exact seam and stopped. So per-metatile attributes belong ON THE TILE via Godot's own custom data, not in a parallel resource keyed by id. (2) **Custom art needs almost no new convention**: reference metatile ids are bounded by the GBA's 10-bit mask (the corridor genuinely reaches 1023) while `MapData.metatile` is `PackedInt32Array`, so custom metatiles take ids ≥ 1024 as extra rows on the same per-pair atlas — `id % 32, id / 32` still resolves and `source_id == plane` is untouched. All 33 atlases are 0.9 MB, so headroom is not a concern. (3) **The two-plane paint is the trap.** A metatile routes to one or two of three planes, so painting a `NORMAL` one into Ground alone renders NOTHING — the identical bug that made half the C3 skirt invisible, waiting for the first person to hand-paint.

**Sub-tiers, in dependency order.** **M27M1 — populate the tile's custom data** (behaviour + layer type at bake time; finishes the half-built seam). **M27M2 — shared per-pair `TileSet` `.tres`**, replacing the copy currently embedded as a SubResource in each scene: 60 shared resources instead of 421, and adding art then updates one file rather than every consumer. **M27M3 — authoring layer + plane routing**: paint into one layer, a `@tool` distributes to the correct planes using the layer type now on the tile. **M27M4 — change detection into the review backlog**: compare painted ids against `MapData.metatile` — which earns its place NOT as a cache of the scene but as the record of what the collision and elevation were last authored AGAINST — then seed via the existing `author_cell_with_defaults()` and clear the explicit bits so the cell surfaces in `review_cells()`. Without it, painting a wall over a path leaves the cell walkable AND still marked confirmed. **M27M5 — new maps from Kanto art**: blank `.tscn` + `.tres` written straight to `scenes/maps/`, no importer and no JSON (`assets/maps/` is gitignored as regenerable output, so authored input cannot live there; §1.9's re-import policy already says *"authored maps simply are not in that set"*). **M27M6 — behaviour picker.** ⚠️ **NO METATILE PICKER WILL BE BUILT — Rob's call, 2026-08-09: *"I don't need the picker if I have the sync button and then later have a behavior overwrite ability."*** A visual palette for choosing ART is redundant: Godot's own tile palette already is one, and `Sync Painted Tiles` (M27M4) makes what you paint with it real. So M27M6 keeps ONLY its original narrow scope — **a named dropdown for choosing a BEHAVIOUR** (240-value enum, plain typed ints with no enum hint; `metatile_behavior.gd` already carries the generated `NAME_BY_ID` table) — and **merges into Part B**, which is the paint mode that applies it. Neither half is useful alone: a dropdown with nothing to paint into, or a brush that takes raw ints.

⚠️ **AND PART B'S PURPOSE IS NARROWED BY THE SAME DECISION.** It is no longer an authoring path — behaviour follows the metatile automatically and is right by construction for every Kanto tile. It is an **OVERRIDE**: the escape hatch for when the derived behaviour is wrong, and the only way to tag behaviour at all on M27M7's new art, which has no sidecar entry. That is a smaller job than "the behaviour brush" sounds, and it is why it sits after `pokemart` rather than before it. **M27M7 — new art**: 16x16 metatiles authored directly (NOT the GBA tiles+metatiles.bin encoding, which exists for hardware constraints that do not apply), appended past id 1023, each tagged once via M27M6.

**What stays a human's job, and why it is irreducible.** Measured across all 421 maps / 230,619 cells: behaviour and layer type vary by placement **0%** — per-metatile by construction, so a painted tile carries them. Collision varies **52.0%** and elevation **52.1%**, so they are properties of the POSITION and cannot be derived from the tile at all. That split is the whole design: tags travel with the tile, collision and elevation get a guess plus a review flag. **New art is the one exception** — a brand-new metatile has no behaviour or layer type anywhere, so both are authored ONCE PER TILE at ingestion, not per placement. Layer type is not derivable even in principle: whether the player walks in front of a tile or behind it is a decision.

**Two questions deliberately left open.** Whether painting directly into `Ground` should be blocked or silently routed; and whether an explicit skirt opt-out flag is worth having (see C3's note — the current empty-`border` mechanism works but cannot distinguish "deliberately none" from "lost its border").

**M27M2 SHIPPED EARLY, 2026-07-30 — it turned out to be the fix for C4's crossing stutter.** Each baked scene embedded its own `TileSet` as a `SubResource`, and building one is **2208 `create_tile()` calls** — a flat ~27 ms per scene load regardless of map size. `map_baker` now writes one shared `TileSet` per tileset PAIR to `assets/map_tilesets/` and references it as an `ext_resource`: **4 files cover the 8-map corridor, 60 would cover all 421**, replacing 421 embedded copies. `take_over_path()` after the save is load-bearing — without it the instance handed to the layers carries no `resource_path` and `ResourceSaver` writes it straight back into the scene as a `SubResource`, sharing nothing. Its original purpose is unchanged: adding custom art now updates one file per pair instead of every consumer.

**[M27M Part C — atlas deduplication] COMPLETE 2026-08-09. Scope of record is `docs/m27m_behaviour_scope.md` §4b; this is the index entry.** The atlases were a flattened composite of primary+secondary, so the primary's 640 metatiles were re-rendered into every pair — 81% of all atlas area. New `scripts/overworld/atlas_layout.gd` is now the ONE place that knows id → (source, coords): **six** TileSet sources per pair (0-2 primary, 3-5 secondary), with the secondary half re-based by 640. Both paint sites (`map_baker._paint`, `map_manager.set_metatile`) call it, and the duplicated `ATLAS_COLS = 32` is deleted from both. **47,424 atlas cells → 28,224; 1.22 MB → 0.63 MB.**

⚠️ **THE DESIGN'S HEADLINE ASSUMPTION — "the primary is stored once per primary tileset" — IS FALSE FOR ONE OF THE TWO PRIMARIES, and it was found by a PIXEL proof, not by the coverage check.** On hardware both tilesets share ONE tile/palette address space, so a primary metatile may name tile index ≥640 or palette slot ≥7 and pick up whatever the *currently paired* secondary put there — a deliberate idiom for a shared building tileset defining a per-building fixture. Rendered to a file it has no single answer. Measured across all 421 converted Kanto maps: **`general_frlg` 0 of 640 borrow; `building_frlg` 56 of 640, and 53 of those are placed, on 208 real cells.**

⚠️ **"BORROWS" IS NOT THE SAME AS "RENDERS WRONG", AND A FIRST REPORT OF THIS CONFLATED THEM.** Screenshot verification (2026-08-09) forced the correction. Three separate narrowings, each measured: (1) a borrowing metatile only renders wrong if it actually differs from the pair that WON THE RACE to write the shared file — alphabetically `building_frlg__generic_building_1_frlg`; (2) across the corridor's 10 building pairs that leaves **6 cells**, not 208; and (3) of those, a difference confined to a plane that a fully-opaque upper plane overdraws is **invisible on screen**. Metatile 98 in the four Pokémon Centres is exactly that case — 254 differing Ground pixels, all 254 overdrawn by an opaque Objects plane — so **the Centres were never visibly wrong, and an earlier claim here that they were is retracted.** The real visible corridor impact is **Silph Co 1F, cells (30,0) and (30,1), metatiles 617/625**: differences in Objects AND Overhangs with 178 and 78 pixels respectively NOT overdrawn. Pre-fix they render **solid black**; post-fix they are the notice board beside the stairwell. Verified by screenshot. Resolved by sharing only where it is shareable: the generator asks `Tileset.primary_borrows_from_secondary()`, a borrowing pair writes its own `<pair>_primary_<plane>.png`, and the baker prefers that name. `AtlasLayout` is untouched — this changes which FILE backs the primary source, never which source or coord an id resolves to. **Cost: the predicted 3.7× is really 1.9×**, since `building_frlg`'s 9 pairs no longer share; `general_frlg`'s 11 still do, and it backs 173,560 of the region's 230,619 cells.

⚠️ **TWO PROOFS SHIP WITH THIS AND EACH CATCHES WHAT THE OTHER CANNOT.** New suite section **AX** walks every painted cell of every baked map (35,156 cells, 32 maps) and asserts the tile it points at EXISTS — break-tested by removing the secondary re-base, which fails it on 628 cells. It did **not** catch the borrowing bug, because a wrongly-shared primary points at a tile that exists and merely holds another building's art; only the pixel proof (render the un-split composite the old way, compare halves) found that. **"Renders nothing" needs a coverage walk; "renders wrong" needs a pixel comparison.** Two expected regressions fixed, not papered over: AR.06/AR.10 asserted three sources and now read `AtlasLayout.SECONDARY_SOURCE_BASE * 2`, and `m27_setmetatile_test` B.03/B.04/B.08/B.09 hardcoded the pre-split mapping for ids 703/704 — which are SECONDARY ids, so a new B.09b pins that so they cannot quietly degrade back. Tests: `m27a_step_resolver_test` 549 → **554/554**, `m27_setmetatile_test` 20 → **21/21**, all 23 other overworld suites green, `check_bake_diff --all` **32/32 reproducible**. ✅ **SCREENSHOT-VERIFIED 2026-08-09** (Rob's request), and it is what forced the correction above. Whole-map captures at 1:1 via a `SubViewport` sized to the map's own pixel extent — needs a real display, which WSLg provides; the headless dummy renderer yields blank images. Pallet Town, Celadon City, Silph Co 1F and both Pokémon Centres all render correctly. **Celadon and Silph Co were baked FOR this check and are new to the corridor (32 → 34 maps)** — both exercise pairs Part C had never rendered, and both confirmed the rule from opposite sides: Celadon is on `general_frlg` and correctly reuses the SHARED primary, while Silph Co is on `building_frlg` and correctly got its OWN. Silph Co is the better fixture of the two — its tileset ships no `tiles.png` or palettes at all and borrows both from Condominiums (the `[M27B]` per-field resolution case), *and* it is the only place in the corridor where the borrowing bug is visible.

**[M27M4 — adopting hand-painted tiles] COMPLETE 2026-08-09.** Closes the gap that made a hand-authored map **render correctly and play inert**: Godot's own TileMap editor writes tiles into the three plane layers and touches `MapData` not at all, while `StepResolver` reads `MapData.behavior`/`collision`/`elevation` — so painted grass was not grass and a painted ledge was not a ledge, with nothing about looking at the map saying so.

**Four Step 0 findings, each of which changed the build.** (1) ⚠️ **It needs an INVERSE of `AtlasLayout`** — hand-painting happens in Godot's editor, which knows only sources and atlas coords, so recovering "which metatile is that" means undoing `source_id()` and `coords()` together. Part C had just made that non-trivial, and a second hand-kept copy would drift silently in BOTH directions (a paint recorded as the wrong metatile, or a correct paint ignored), so `AtlasLayout.metatile_id()` lives beside its own forward rules. (2) ⚠️ **`snapshot_cells()` captured FOUR arrays and this tier writes SIX** — an undo would have reverted a sync HALF WAY, restoring the old collision/elevation/review state while keeping the new metatile and behaviour, a combination nothing produces legitimately and the overlay draws as if fine. Exactly the failure that docstring already warned about for the explicit bits, arriving through a new writer; `restore_cells` reads the two new keys defensively so an undo action captured before this session still applies. (3) **The scope line "pulling behaviour off the tile" is stale** — the `behavior` custom-data layer is still declared by `map_baker` and **still never populated**; Part A shipped the per-pair SIDECAR instead, so behaviour resolves through `MapManager.behavior_for()`. The inert layer remains the open item it already was. (4) **`MapData.metatile` is a WATERMARK, not a cache** — nothing renders from it, and what it records is what the collision and elevation were last authored AGAINST, which is precisely why a disagreement with the scene is meaningful rather than noise.

**A PULL, not a hook, deliberately**: Godot emits no "the user painted a tile" signal, and polling the scene per frame in the editor is the same per-cell cost that made the collision-paint path stall ~5 s — so a **Sync Painted Tiles** button sits beside Save Map Data, and Godot's own TileMap undo never fights this addon's over one gesture. The rule lives on `MapOverlay` (testable); the plugin only presses it, wrapped in the same snapshot/restore undo action painting already uses.

⚠️ **THE RE-AUTHORING IS THE POINT, NOT THE METATILE WRITE.** Collision and elevation cannot be derived from a tile — 52.0% and 52.1% of Kanto metatiles appear with more than one value — so a repainted cell is carrying a guess made about the tile that USED to be there. Sync therefore calls `author_cell_with_defaults()`, clearing both explicit bits so the cell lands in `review_cells()`. **`set_metatile_id` deliberately does NOT mark provenance on its own**, or every sync would make a map refuse to re-bake without `--force`. Two outcomes are reported rather than resolved, because either answer would be a guess: a cell showing DIFFERENT metatiles on two planes (a hand-paint no brush produces), and a metatile the pair has no behaviour for — where **-1 is kept distinct from `MB_NORMAL` (0)**, which is 62% of the region and would make a missing table look like ordinary ground.

**Tests**: section AY, 14 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 553 → 567, driven against the real baked Pallet Town rather than a fixture. **Five injections, each failing exactly what it should and nothing else**: dropping the re-author fails AY.11; reading only the Ground plane fails AY.06/AY.07/AY.14 (62% of Kanto is NORMAL and puts nothing on ground); the pre-M27M4 four-array snapshot fails AY.13; silently resolving a conflict fails AY.14; forgetting to re-base the secondary half in the inverse fails 7. ⚠️ **Two of this section's own drafts were wrong before the code was** — one wrote to the fixture and then asserted the map was untouched, the other repainted a SINGLE plane and expected adoption when the correct answer is a conflict. Both are recorded at the assertions, since a test that paints something no brush produces proves nothing about painting.

**[M27M3 — the metatile brush] COMPLETE 2026-08-09.** `EditMode.METATILE` plus a `paint_metatile` id: click a cell and the tile changes AND its rules follow, in one gesture. Small tier, because the rule already existed — `set_metatile` has routed a metatile into the three planes since the `setmetatile` opcode landed; what is new is a human reaching it.

⚠️ **TWO STEP 0 FINDINGS CONTRADICTED THE RESCOPE'S OWN PREMISE, and both are recorded rather than quietly implemented around.** The 2026-08-08 rescope says the brush *"writes all three planes at the cell … so it costs nothing and needs no routing lookup"*. (1) **The lookup is no longer needed-but-absent — Part A shipped it.** `<pair>_layer_types.json` covers all 60 pairs and `MapManager._layer_type_for` is already what `set_metatile` routes through, so the premise that routing data was unavailable to the editor has expired. (2) **Painting all three planes and routing are NOT equivalent, and the difference is stale art.** Repainting a COVERED cell with a NORMAL metatile must ERASE the ground plane; a blanket three-plane write leaves whatever was there. So the brush ROUTES, and — the point — it routes by calling the same function the opcode does. `MapManager.paint_metatile()` was extracted for exactly that: a brush that routed differently from the runtime opcode would be `check_bake_diff`-vs-`map_baker` again, one rule kept by hand in two places, in a place where the symptom is a map that merely looks right.

⚠️ **AN UNROUTABLE ID NOW REFUSES INSTEAD OF ERASING.** `_layer_type_for` answers -1 for a pair it has no table for, and the extracted-from code fed that straight into `ROUTING.get(lt, [])` — an empty route, which erases all three planes. Silent, and it deletes art. Nothing at that point can know where the metatile belongs, so refusing is the only honest answer.

⚠️ **AND THE SAME HALF-WAY UNDO AS M27M4, ONE LEVEL UP.** This is the first edit that changes the SCENE as well as the data, so a snapshot of the data alone would undo the rules and leave the picture painted — a cell drawn as grass that the game reads as whatever preceded it. `snapshot_cells` now also carries each layer's `tile_map_data` blob, keeping it symmetric with the whole-array approach rather than inventing a per-cell tile delta. `adopt_cell()` is shared with M27M4's sync button, so a cell cannot play differently depending on which way it was painted.

**Tests**: section AZ, 9 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 567 → 576, on the real baked Pallet Town with a target metatile chosen to have DIFFERENT routing from what is already there — a same-routing fixture cannot tell a routed brush from a three-plane one. Four injections, each failing exactly what it should: painting all three planes (the rescope's own proposal) fails AZ.04; restoring `ROUTING.get(lt, [])` fails AZ.08; dropping the tile snapshot fails AZ.09; painting without adopting fails AZ.05/06/07.

**[M27M5 — authored maps, and the first one: Xanadu Nursery] COMPLETE 2026-08-09.** The first map this project INVENTS rather than imports. `MapAuthoring` (create / fill / fill_rect_quad / add_connection / save_map) writes a `.tscn` + `_data.tres` straight to `scenes/maps/` — **no importer and no JSON, deliberately**: `assets/maps/` is gitignored as regenerable output, so authored input cannot live there, and the scene IS the source, the same call M27M already made for tiles.

⚠️ **THE BLOCKER WAS NOT THE FILE FORMAT — IT WAS THAT A NEW MAP COULD NOT BE NAMED.** `MapData.connections` and `Warp.dest_map` both store a `MAP_*` constant, and every consumer resolves through `MapConstants`, which is GENERATED from the reference's 939 `map.json` files. Measured before building anything: `is_baked()` answers false for any name the reference does not define, so `loadable_connections()` **silently drops the edge** — no error, no warning, the neighbour simply never loads. New hand-owned `AuthoredMaps` holds `MAP_AUTHORED_<NAME> -> <MapName>`, and the generator now emits a `map_name_for` that DELEGATES to it on a miss. ⚠️ Two files with two owners on purpose: an authored map added to the generated table would be erased by the next `gen_map_import.py` run, silently — the `metatile_behavior.gd` failure exactly. The prefix mirrors Q4's `FLAG_AUTHORED_` convention and is clean **by measurement**: zero of the reference's 939 constants begin `MAP_AUTHORED_`.

**`map_baker.build_map_scene()` was EXTRACTED rather than copied.** The five-node layout is not decoration — `MapManager` finds planes by NAME, `z_index` comes from `PLANE_Z`, and the two entity strata straddle the overhang plane because source's own `sElevationToPriority` puts elevation-4 entities above it and everything else below. A second builder that got any of that subtly wrong would load, render, and misorder its sprites.

⚠️ **AND `check_bake_diff` WAS REPORTING AUTHORED MAPS AS "REPRODUCIBLE", WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF TRUE.** With no importer input the baker simply fails, the tracked scene is left untouched, the diff finds no drift — so the project's own acceptance tool silently blessed the one class of map it cannot check. It now partitions them out by name and says so, while still uid-checking them, because a missing `uid` is a real defect however a scene was made (`ResourceSaver` writes none; `MapAuthoring` reuses the baker's own `_preserve_or_mint_uid`).

**Xanadu Nursery**: 20x18 on `general_frlg__viridian_city_frlg` — Route 2's own pair, so the seam is visually continuous — tree wall two cells thick, walkable interior at elevation 3, and a two-row doorway on its EAST edge at y=4-5. Linked reciprocally: Route 2 gains WEST -> `MAP_AUTHORED_XANADU_NURSERY` offset 0, Xanadu gains EAST -> `MAP_ROUTE2` offset 0. Verified live rather than assumed: it loads as a real neighbour at origin **(-20, 0)**, and global (-1, 4) is owned by Xanadu — directly against Route 2's (0, 4). **Offset 0 was chosen deliberately**: it keeps the reciprocal offsets equal AND keeps Xanadu clear of Pewter City, which sits at (-12, -40) and would otherwise OVERLAP — and `chunk_owning()` is first-match-wins over an unordered Dictionary, so overlapping chunks answer nondeterministically.

⚠️ **ROUTE 2's WEST WALL IS STILL SOLID — all 80 rows of x=0, measured — so the link is not yet walkable.** Rob is painting the doorway by hand (his call), which is what M27M3's brush exists for: cells **(0,4) (1,4) (0,5) (1,5)** to metatile **8** at elevation **3**. ⚠️ Until then Route 2 carries a connection but no AUTHORED cell, so the baker's re-import guard does NOT protect it and a `--force` re-bake would drop the link; painting the doorway is what arms that guard.

**Tests**: section BA, 9 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 576 → 585. Two injections, each failing exactly what it should: a regeneration that forgot to delegate fails BA.02/BA.04; a reciprocal offset recorded with the wrong sign fails BA.06. BA.07 computes the seam through the real `neighbour_origin` rather than eyeballing it.

**[M27M5 — FIRST HAND-AUTHORED CONTENT, and the corridor's first authored map is WALKABLE] 2026-08-09.** Rob painted the Route 2 doorway with M27M3's brush and walked into Xanadu Nursery — the first time in this project that a map it invented has been entered in play. Cells (0,4) (1,4) (0,5) (1,5) at collision 0; (0,4)/(0,5) also re-tiled to metatile 13, which correctly picked up `MB_TALL_GRASS` from the pair sidecar with no behaviour painting involved, because behaviour follows the metatile.

⚠️ **A STALE CLAIM IS IN COMMITTED HISTORY AND IS CORRECTED HERE, NOT REWRITTEN.** Commit `3158502f`'s message says *"Their collision is still 1 … and the doorway is not yet walkable."* That was true when the diff was analysed and false by the time it was committed — Rob finished the collision pass in between, and the message was not re-checked against the tree before writing. The commit is otherwise accurate; this note is the correction. **Re-read state before describing it, especially when the other party is editing the same files concurrently.**

⚠️ **Elevation on those cells is 0, not 3, and that is CORRECT rather than an oversight** — `IsElevationMismatchAt` treats 0 and 15 as wildcards, so a 0 cell never mismatches the pocket's elevation 3. A recommendation to set 3 was given and is unnecessary. Cosmetic and left to Rob: (1,4)/(1,5) keep their original TREE metatiles (31/23) with collision cleared, so the gap is walked through art that still reads as trees.

**[M27M5c — the authoring dialogs] SCOPED 2026-08-10, Phase 1 built.** The GUI surface `map_creator.gd` has always said it was waiting for: *"⚠️ THIS IS THE TEST SURFACE, NOT THE USER SURFACE. The rules live on `MapAuthoring` and the editor dialog presses the same functions"* (`:6-9`). The CLI is the headless-testable twin — the same split `map_baker`/`check_bake_diff` already use — and the dialog was always the intended human path. It simply had not been built.

⚠️ **SCOPED ONLY AFTER READING THE OVERLAY, WHICH IS FURTHER ALONG THAN TWO ROUNDS OF ADVICE HAD ASSUMED.** An earlier answer in the same conversation described a region-graph connection view as "a much bigger build" and recommended deferring it. It is substantially **already built** and had to be retracted: `map_overlay.gd`'s M27M5 connections view draws every placed map at its computed origin, **highlights overlaps** via `intersects`, instantiates real neighbour previews (`_sync_previews`), and edits a seam's offset with the reciprocal written to the neighbour and saved neighbours-first (`_apply_offset`/`_dirty_neighbours`/`_save_map_data`). **Read the surface before sizing work on it** — the same lesson `[M27R Step 1]`'s grep-vs-measure correction already records, arrived at from the tooling side.

**So the real gap is four items, not a subsystem**: adding a connection (`connect_maps()` has zero GUI callers), removing one (no path either side), creating a map at all (CLI only), and changing an existing seam's direction/destination (only offset is editable).

**Phases.** **1 — New Map dialog** (built, see below). **2 — Add Connection dialog**: host is the open map, guest an `OptionButton` over baked maps, direction + offset; calls `connect_maps()`, which already returns `{ok, reason, overlaps}` so a refusal can be **shown and named** rather than scrolling past in a console — strictly better than the CLI, and the overlay's existing highlight then points at it. **3 — Remove Connection**: the one genuinely NEW rule, a `MapAuthoring.disconnect_maps()` dropping both sides, belonging on `MapAuthoring` with assertions in section BB beside `connect_maps`/`would_overlap`. Direction/destination editing is deliberately **delete-and-re-add** rather than its own editor — cheaper and less to get wrong.

⚠️ **THE GOTCHA FOR THE COMBINED CREATE-THEN-CONNECT FLOW, and it is already solved — do not re-break it.** `register_constant()` writes to `authored_maps.gd`, whose `NAME_BY_CONSTANT` is a `const` compiled into the running process, so **a constant just written is invisible until a rescan**. In the CLI that was an edge case; in a dialog that creates and then connects in one session it is the NORMAL path. `_constant_of` already carries a third lookup that reads the file back for exactly this, and `connect_maps` fails loudly (*"has no MAP_* constant — register it first"*) if it regresses.

**Constraints, all inherited rather than invented.** Rules live on `MapAuthoring` and the dialogs hold **none** — stated outright by both `map_creator.gd:6-9` and `name_usage_dialog.gd:5-11`, and the reason is recorded: this addon is the project's one surface with no automated coverage, so a rule-free dialog is what keeps it safe to leave untested. Writes stay on **explicit buttons** (the `EditorFileSystem` rescan is the ~5 s stall that forced Save Map Data to exist). Two-sided writes reuse `_dirty_neighbours` rather than saving inline.

**[M27M5c Phase 1 — the New Map dialog] COMPLETE 2026-08-10.** A **New Map** button beside Save Map Data / Sync Painted Tiles / Legend / Name Usage / Overlay, opening a dialog that creates, registers and opens an authored map without a command line.

**A pure shell over already-tested functions**, which is the whole reason it is low-risk on a surface with no coverage: `usable_pairs()` fills the tileset dropdown, `default_fill_for()` derives the fill so **no metatile id has to be known or typed**, `map_exists()` refuses an overwrite, `create_map()`/`save_map()` write the pair of artifacts, `register_constant()` adds the `MAP_AUTHORED_*` entry. Every one is driven by `m27a_step_resolver_test` section BB already.

⚠️ **THE PAIR DROPDOWN IS THE PART THAT MAKES THIS USABLE, AND IT IS SMALL FOR A REAL REASON.** A pair is only listed once a map on it has been **baked** — that is what writes the shared TileSet — so `usable_pairs()` returns 16 of 60 rather than everything. That is what lets this be a plain `OptionButton` with no typeahead, unlike the trainer-key (623) / script-label (17,159) / graphics-id (387) pickers that needed one. The dialog says so in its own hint rather than leaving an empty-looking list unexplained.

**Opens the new scene on success** (`EditorInterface.open_scene_from_path`), so creating lands you in the map ready to paint rather than hunting for it in the FileSystem dock — which is the difference between this and the CLI it replaces.

⚠️ **NOT TEST-COVERED, AND THAT IS THE ESTABLISHED CONVENTION RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** The dialog holds no rules, so there is nothing in it a test could assert that section BB does not already cover; the addon has no automated coverage by design and has shipped three defects historically. **It has not been clicked** — verified only by a clean `--import` parse and by the `MapAuthoring` suites staying green. Rob's own hands-on pass is what confirms it, exactly as it was for the freeze/undo fixes.

**[M27M5c Phase 2 — the Connect Map dialog] COMPLETE 2026-08-10.** A **Connect Map** button stitching the OPEN map to another, with the reciprocal edge and offset derived and both `_data.tres` written.

**The host is the open scene, not a third dropdown.** `connect_maps` is symmetric — it writes both sides — so a host picker would be a second way to express one edit and an invitation to connect two maps while looking at a third. `_apply_offset` already makes exactly this assumption for the seam editor beside it.

**What it adds over `map_creator.tscn --connect` is that A REFUSAL IS SHOWN.** `connect_maps` already returns `{ok, reason, overlaps}`, so *"would overlap Pewter City"* arrives in the box with the colliding maps named — and the overlay's own `_draw_connections` already highlights overlaps in the viewport, so the reason and the picture agree. On the CLI that reason scrolls past in a console. `force` is exposed, off by default, labelled with the real consequence rather than the word alone: an overlap makes `chunk_owning()` first-match-wins over an unordered Dictionary, so it reproduces intermittently.

⚠️ **A REAL HAZARD FOUND WHILE BUILDING IT, WARNED RATHER THAN BLOCKED.** `connect_maps` reloads both maps through `load()`, which returns Godot's CACHED resource — the very instance the overlay has been mutating — and then saves it. So connecting while the overlay holds pending offset edits would silently commit them under the guise of adding a seam. The dialog checks `has_unsaved_edits()` on open and says so. **Warned, not refused**: committing them is usually what was wanted anyway, and refusing outright would strand an author with no way forward from inside the dialog.

**Two small additions, both deliberately OUTSIDE the dialog** so they are testable on a surface that has coverage: `MapAuthoring.baked_maps(exclude)` (the guest list — BAKED, not defined, since `MapConstants` knows 939 constants but only the ~35 with a real scene can be stitched to, and `loadable_connections()` drops the rest at runtime anyway) and `MapOverlay.refresh_connections()` (invalidates `_conn_cache` and re-syncs previews so a new seam draws immediately rather than after a reselect — ⚠️ deliberately does NOT touch `_unsaved_edits`, since `connect_maps` has already saved and claiming a pending edit would make the next Save write an unchanged file and fire the warning above on a clean tree).

⚠️ **ONE DEVIATION FROM THIS BLOCK'S OWN SCOPE, MADE ON PURPOSE.** The scope said two-sided writes should reuse `_dirty_neighbours` rather than saving inline. `connect_maps` already saves inline and is tested that way; re-plumbing it would change tested behaviour so a dedicated **Connect** press would then *not* connect until a separate Save. The real constraint — no writes on change, only on an explicit button — is satisfied either way. `_dirty_neighbours` remains right for incremental offset nudges; a one-shot connect is a different gesture.

**Tests: section BB 12 → 16 assertions** (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 617 → 621), covering only `baked_maps` — the phase's whole testable surface, since the dialogs hold no rules. **Break-tested**: making it ignore `exclude` fails BB.15 and nothing else. Regression: `m27_setmetatile_test` 21/21, `m27n_weather_test` 54/54, `m27f_script_vm_test` 311/311, `m27g_integration_test` 22/22, no baked-scene contamination. ⚠️ **Neither dialog has been clicked** — verified by clean `--import` and by a real headless editor boot (which is what catches a bad `preload` path or wrong container constant, unlike a parse check). Rob's hands-on pass is what confirms them.

**[M27M5c Phase 3 — removing a seam] COMPLETE 2026-08-10. M27M5c IS CLOSED.** New `MapAuthoring.disconnect_maps()` — the inverse of `connect_maps`, dropping both sides — plus a **Remove** control in the same dialog, which is now **"Map connections"** (and its toolbar button **"Connections"**) rather than "Connect Map": it answers *"what does this map border"* from both ends, and two buttons each showing half of that would be worse.

⚠️ **KEYED ON (direction, guest), AND DIRECTION ALONE WOULD BE WRONG — measured, not assumed.** Across all **939 reference `map.json` files**: **3 maps carry more than one connection on a single edge** — `SixIsland_WaterPath_Frlg` has **three** on its left, plus `Route111` and `Route124` — so "remove this map's WEST connection" is genuinely ambiguous there and would drop whichever sorted first. The same sweep found **zero** `(direction, map)` pairs repeated on one map, so the pair is unique everywhere the data exists. The 34-map corridor contains **none** of the 3, so this is exactly the class of thing that ships fine and breaks on the next import — the multi-seam case is therefore SYNTHESISED in the tests rather than waited for.

⚠️ **A MISSING RECIPROCAL IS REPORTED, NOT FATAL.** If the guest has no matching edge the two sides had already drifted; removing only the host's side is still an improvement, and refusing would leave an author unable to clear a half-seam through any tool at all. `removed_host`/`removed_guest` come back separately so the dialog says which happened — reporting a drifted pair as a clean unlink would hide that the data was inconsistent.

**Tests: section BB 16 → 20** (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 621 → 625). ⚠️ **Driven through the PURE `_drop_connection` on DUPLICATED/synthetic `MapData`, never through `disconnect_maps`** — BB.08's own hard-won rule, that breaking a guard inside a WRITING function makes the injection perform the very write the test exists to prevent, which is how that assertion corrupted two tracked `.tres` files on its first draft. **Two injections, each failing exactly what it should**: direction-only keying fails BB.18 **and** BB.19; forward iteration while removing fails BB.19 **and** BB.20 (and additionally throws out-of-bounds, which is the bug itself). Regression: setmetatile 21/21, weather 54/54, integration 22/22, editor boots clean, **no `_data.tres` written by any test**.

**Still not clicked** — all three dialogs are verified by parse, editor boot and the rules beneath them, never by use. Also deliberately absent: editing an existing seam's **direction or destination**, which stays delete-and-re-add (cheaper, and less to get wrong than a mutation path that has to re-derive a reciprocal mid-edit).

**[M27R 7c cries + 7a-3 battle SFX] COMPLETE 2026-08-09.** Two of Phase 3's three remaining items; only BGM is left, and it is blocked on Rob's asset folder.

**7c — 386 cries, dex-keyed, 3.8 MB.** New `gen_cries.py` pulls one per roster species into `assets/audio/cries/cry_%04d.wav`, `AudioMap.cry_path(dex)` resolves it, `FieldAudio.play_cry(dex)` plays it on the shared SE pool (source plays cries on the same DirectSound voices; a dedicated channel would let a cry overlap an effect source would have cut short), and **`playmoncry` stops being a no-op.**

⚠️ **FROM THE REFERENCE TREE, NOT THE VENDORED ESSENTIALS PACK.** Both carry cries — Essentials 654 as `ABRA.wav`, the reference 1,161 as `abra.wav` — and the reference's are the authentic GBA rips, measured **8-bit mono 10512 Hz**, which is DirectSound's own format. ⚠️ **A CORRECTION TO AN EARLIER CLAIM IN THIS SESSION**: the Essentials `Audio/Cries/` path is EMPTY; its cries live under `Audio/SE/Cries/`. The 20 MB full set makes this a SELECTIVE pull, unlike `[M27D D1]`'s all-449 sprite pull at 0.5 MB — the work list comes from `pokemon.json`, so a growing roster re-runs rather than being hand-kept.

⚠️ **THE GENDERED NIDORAN ARE THE ONE REAL TRAP, and it is `[M27B Step 4]`'s collision again.** `pokemon.json` spells them `Nidoran♀`/`Nidoran♂`, and any normaliser that strips non-alphanumerics collapses BOTH to `nidoran` — silently giving one of them the other's cry. The sign is mapped BEFORE anything is stripped, and G.03 asserts the two files differ by CONTENT rather than by name. Break-tested: dropping the mapping makes the generator fail and name both.

⚠️ **AND THE GENERATOR SUCCEEDED AT DOING NOTHING ON ITS FIRST RUN.** A guessed `national_dex_num` key made every row skip, and it printed `gen_cries: 0 cries` as though that were a result. A nonzero floor now fails the build. The species-name probe had passed, which is why the key went unchecked — **a probe that validates one field does not validate the row.**

**7a-3 — the 11 battle effects**, catalogued in the shared `AudioMap` because the battle screen is shared with the simulator. Two rules extracted rather than buried in the screen, so both are assertable: `damage_se(effectiveness)` — ⚠️ **a super-effective hit that sounds resisted is the most audible mistake a battle can make**, so above 1 is super, below is weak, exactly 1 is normal, and an immune hit makes NO damage sound because no damage happened — and `catch_sequence(shakes, caught)`, which is **`[M27H H4]`'s recorded shake count finally acquiring a consumer**: 0-2 shakes ends on the ball opening, 3 on the click. ⚠️ H.02 checks every name resolves to a REAL FILE, because catalogued is not present — that is how `SE_SAVE` once ended up aimed at the ME folder.

**Tests**: `m27r_audio_test` 40 → 56 (sections G and H). Three injections: inverting super/weak fails H.03, ending a capture on the wrong sound fails H.06, dropping the Nidoran mapping fails the BUILD. ⚠️ **NOT HEARD** — every assertion is on cue records and file existence; no sound has been played through a device. **And the battle hooks are NOT wired yet**: the catalogue and the selection rules exist, `battle_screen_shared.gd` does not call them. That is deliberate — the rules are the part a suite can hold, and wiring them is a screen change belonging with M26B7's catch animation.

**[M27R 7a-3b — the battle SFX wiring] COMPLETE 2026-08-10. Battles make noise.** `[M27R 7c]` shipped the catalogue and the two selection rules and stopped there, deliberately — `battle_screen_shared.gd` referenced none of it. This is the calling half: six hooks, one new beat kind.

⚠️ **A SOUND IS A BEAT, NOT A SIGNAL HANDLER, and this is the finding the whole shape rests on.** The battle screen does not render a turn as it happens — it QUEUES `_pending_beats` and drains them sequentially with `await`. Playing a sound directly from `move_executed` fires it SECONDS before the damage it belongs to is drawn, so every sound in a turn would stack up at the front while the visuals played out after. A tenth beat kind, `"sfx"`, queued in order with everything else. ⚠️ It is **fire-and-forget — deliberately NOT awaited**, unlike every other beat: a sound is not a beat you wait for.

⚠️ **THE DAMAGE SOUND IS QUEUED BEFORE THE `hp_drain` BEAT, AND THAT IS AN ASSERTION.** The hit is heard as the bar STARTS moving rather than after it settles. Injecting the swap fails the ordering assertion *and* a pre-existing end-to-end sequence check, which is the stronger signal.

**`FieldAudio` is renamed `GameAudio` and reused by both halves** (D1, Rob's call) — nothing in it was overworld-specific but its name and an unused BGM stub, and a second SE pool with a second lifetime is the two-hand-kept-copies shape this project has paid for repeatedly. The rename touches `overworld.gd`, `script_vm.gd`, `field_native_events.gd` and `m27r_audio_test.gd` — **including the suite that is the only proof any of this works**, so the acceptance check was its own: **57/57 before and after, same count**. One orphaned `field_audio.gd.uid` was left tracked by the move and removed (nothing referenced its uid; grepped before deleting, per the `[M27M]` incident where an `rm` of `.uid` files took tracked files with it).

**The SFX bus is the point of the bus** (D3): `BUS_NAME = "SFX"` at a fixed `BUS_DB`, created at runtime by `_ensure_bus()` and routed to Master, with every SE voice, the ME player and the BGM player set to it — so a later options screen or a persisted setting changes ONE value rather than auditing every `play_se` call.

**Six hooks**: damage (one site, all three variants, `_pending_hit_effectiveness` already set by `_on_hit_effectiveness_computed` which runs ahead of it), the faint recall and the switch-out recall, send-out, and ⚠️ **flee ONLY ON A SUCCESSFUL ESCAPE** — a failed flee is a real outcome that costs the turn, and sounding the escape jingle for it would say the opposite of what happened.

⚠️ **TESTS ASSERT THE QUEUE, NEVER PLAYBACK, and that is forced rather than chosen.** `_run_message_pacing()` returns early and clears the queue for `--autoplay` and for off-tree instances — exactly the conditions a headless suite runs in — so asserting a sound PLAYED would be asserting through a path deliberately switched off here.

⚠️ **ONE ASSERTION WAS FOUND VACUOUS BY ITS OWN INJECTION — rule 13 again.** The no-damage guard originally used an IMMUNE hit (damage 0 AND effectiveness 0), which is refused twice over: `damage_se()` returns `""` **and** the drain block is skipped. Removing the empty-name guard failed nothing. Rebuilt on a NEUTRAL effectiveness, which names a real sound, so only the no-damage gate can suppress it; the empty-name half is now asserted where it IS the deciding rule. Re-injected: the call moved outside the `damage > 0` gate now fails it.

**Tests**: `message_pacing_test` 70 → **92/92**. Six injections, each failing exactly what it should: an accepted empty name → 1, the sound after the drain → 2, one fixed damage sound regardless of effectiveness → 4, no send-out → 2, no faint recall → 3, the damage sound outside the `damage > 0` gate → 2. Regression: **all 25 overworld suites green** through `run_overworld_tests.sh` (`m27a_step_resolver_test` 617/617 down through `m27r_audio_test` 57/57, no engine ERROR lines) plus 9 battle-side suites and both `--autoplay` runs 1/1. ⚠️ Worth recording: **`m27h_catching_test` no longer hangs** — it ran clean at 57/57, so the pre-existing hang this file flags repeatedly appears to be gone.

⚠️ **NOT COVERED, AND STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED.** The **flee hook has no test** — it sits inside the Run button's own handler behind `_bm.try_flee()`, which needs a real wild battle to reach, while the suite drives bare instances. And **nothing has been heard**: every assertion is on beat records, no sound has been played through a device. The four ball sounds stay with **M26B7** per D2, so they can be timed once against the animation they describe rather than guessed and re-tuned.

**[M27M Part B — the behaviour override, absorbing M27M6] COMPLETE 2026-08-09.** `EditMode.BEHAVIOR` plus a `paint_behavior` **named dropdown**, built from the generated `NAME_BY_ID` table so it cannot drift from source — 240 plain ints with no enum hint was the complaint that opened M27M6, and this is the only part of it built.

⚠️ **IT IS AN OVERRIDE, NOT AN AUTHORING PATH, AND THE EXPLICIT BIT IS THE WHOLE TIER.** Behaviour follows the metatile automatically and is right by construction for every Kanto tile (0% placement variance, measured). Part B exists for the two cases that cannot work: a tile whose derived meaning is wrong for one placement, and M27M7's new art, which has no sidecar entry. New `AttrFlag.BEHAVIOR_EXPLICIT` records "a human decided this", and **`adopt_cell` refuses to re-derive a cell carrying it** — without that, Sync would silently revert a hand-set meaning on the next press of a button whose whole job is to be pressed often, and the map would look unchanged while playing differently.

⚠️ **`BEHAVIOR_EXPLICIT` IS DELIBERATELY NOT IN `ATTR_ALL_EXPLICIT`.** `needs_review` is `AUTHORED and not ATTR_ALL_EXPLICIT`, so folding it in would mark every authored cell in the project as needing review the instant this shipped, burying the collision and elevation guesses that genuinely need looking at. Confirmed by injection: folding it in fails **J.19 and P.21**, two assertions that predate this tier.

⚠️ **THE METATILE BRUSH IS HIDDEN, NOT REMOVED — and it was removed first.** Rob asked for removal, then for hiding, then removal, then hiding again; the shipped answer is hiding, and the round trip is recorded because the removal was fully implemented and reverted. `_validate_property` gives `paint_metatile` `PROPERTY_USAGE_STORAGE` (serialised and script-reachable, absent from the Inspector) and omits `METATILE` from `edit_mode`'s hint string without touching the enum — so the brush still works, a scene already set to it keeps working, and a future picker un-hides it by deleting two lines. `BEHAVIOR` is appended at **5**, after `METATILE` at 4, so no serialised `edit_mode` is re-pointed. **`MapManager.paint_metatile` was never in question** — it is the shared routing rule the runtime `setmetatile` opcode runs on.

**Tests**: section BD, 7 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 603 → 610. Four injections, each failing exactly what it should: a re-sync clobbering the override fails BD.05; writing the value without the bit fails BD.03/04/05; folding the bit into `ATTR_ALL_EXPLICIT` fails BD.02 plus the two older assertions above; leaving the brush visible fails BD.07.

**[M27M5b C1/C2/C3 — the map creator] COMPLETE 2026-08-09. Scope of record: `docs/m27m5_map_creator_scope.md`, all five decisions answered there.** Making and connecting a map no longer needs a hand-written driver. `scenes/overworld/map_creator.tscn` takes `--name/--pair/--size/--fill/--connect/--force`, and with no arguments lists what a map can be built on.

**C1 create.** ⚠️ **A pair is usable only once a map on it has been BAKED** — that is what writes the shared `TileSet`. Measured: 60 pairs region-wide, 22 with rendered atlases, **16 with a TileSet, exactly the set the 35 baked maps use**. Not a defect, just the vertical slice, but it is the first wall anyone hits, so the tool names all 16 and the command to unlock another. **The fill metatile is DERIVED** (most common walkable/plain/elevation-3 tile on that pair), so a map can be created without knowing a single metatile id — which matters more now that no metatile picker is being built (see M27M6). It reads BAKED `_data.tres`, never `assets/maps/*.json`, which is gitignored and may be absent.

**C2 connect.** Reciprocal direction and offset are **derived, never given**. ⚠️ Overlap is checked against **every map reachable from the host**, not the direct neighbour — placement is not a property of one edge, and Pewter City sits two hops from Xanadu at (-12,-40), which is precisely the collision that had to be avoided by hand. Refused rather than warned, because `chunk_owning()` is first-match-wins over an unordered Dictionary and an overlap reproduces intermittently.

**C3 authored encounters.** `data/authored_encounters.json`, hand-owned, merged on a miss — ⚠️ **JSON rather than `MapData` fields, Rob's call, and it follows this project's own two-layer rule: full dataset in JSON, implemented BEHAVIOUR in `.tres`.** A recommendation for `MapData` fields was overridden and the doc records why the override is better: every other encounter table is already JSON, and a second storage shape for one kind of data is drift arriving disguised as a convenience. The one real objection — a name key detaches silently on rename — is answered by `unresolved_authored_maps()`, which turns it into a named failure. Xanadu Nursery is seeded with Route 2's own table, so its grass works.

⚠️ **THREE BUGS THE BUILD FOUND, TWO OF THEM IN MY OWN TEST DESIGN.**
(1) **`_constant_of` could not see a constant just written.** `AuthoredMaps.NAME_BY_CONSTANT` is a `const` compiled into the running process, so a line `register_constant` had written to DISK was invisible until an editor rescan — and the creator does exactly create-then-connect, so it refused to link a map it had registered one line earlier. Fixed with a third lookup that reads the file back; deliberately a text match on the real entry rather than trusting `constant_for()`, which would happily invent a constant for a map nobody registered.
(2) **The derived fill was unstable.** An early draft stopped at the first baked map it found and answered `371` for `general_frlg__viridian_city_frlg` where the whole-corpus measurement says `8` — a valid tile chosen by `DirAccess` ordering. A default that depends on directory order is not a default.
(3) ⚠️ **BREAK-TESTING A REFUSAL GUARD PERFORMED THE WRITE IT WAS REFUSING.** BB.08 drove the real `connect_maps`; with the overlap check injected out, the call stopped refusing and went ahead and **wrote spurious connections into two tracked `.tres` files**. A read-only test silently became a writing one. The decision is now a pure `would_overlap()` that `connect_maps` calls, and the assertion drives that — **assert the decision, not the act**. A separate injection on `register_constant`'s duplicate guard did the same thing in miniature, writing a duplicate entry into the hand-owned file, which is the corruption that guard exists to prevent.

**Tests**: section BB, 12 assertions, `EXPECTED_TOTAL` 585 → 597. Three injections, each failing exactly one assertion **and dirtying zero files**: a host-only overlap check fails BB.08, dropping the authored-table fallback fails BB.09, and a stale name in the encounters file fails BB.11.

**Freeze and undo — BOTH FIXED, and they were ONE defect, not two.** Rob's hands-on pass reported Ctrl+Z doing nothing and a ~5 s editor stall per collision paint. **Measured before deciding anything**: raw `ResourceSaver.save()` on the real artifacts costs **0.17 / 0.44 / 0.83 ms** for 480 / 1680 / 3726 cells — the stall was **~5000× the write**, so the write itself was never the cost; `snapshot_cells()` is ~0.001 ms and never a factor; no memory pressure (11 GiB free, 0 B swap), closing the WSLg branch. The cost was editor-side work triggered BY the write (`EditorFileSystem` rescan across 30,741 watched files, 21,979 of them `.import` sidecars). `EditorFileSystem.update_file()` was tried as the surgical fix and **did not suppress it**. **Resolved by taking the write off the paint path entirely** — and the undo defect dissolved with it: a working Ctrl+Z had been stalling exactly as long as a paint, so it read as "nothing happened". Verified live: `pre_drag=4 post=4`, one `map_data` id throughout, `restore_cells` firing on `Scene Undo`. **Both defects confirmed fixed by Rob's own hands-on pass, 2026-07-29: no lag, and Ctrl+Z reverting correctly.** That confirmation matters more than usual here — the plugin is this project's one surface with no automated coverage, so an editor-only behaviour has no other way to be checked.

**The save affordance and the dirty indicator ship in the SAME change, because either alone is a trap.** `paint()` sets `_unsaved_edits`; a loud `UNSAVED EDITS — use Save Map Data in the 2D toolbar` banner draws only while dirty; the plugin owns a **Save Map Data** button in `CONTAINER_CANVAS_EDITOR_MENU` — deliberately its own control rather than leaning on Ctrl+S, since the normal scene-save path is exactly what once put a `MapOverlay` node into a baked map and made Section N necessary. `restore_cells()` now dirties rather than saves: an undo moves memory AWAY from disk just as a paint does, so the indicator must come ON. **Deliberately NOT debounced** — a stall that fires whenever you pause to think is worse than one you can predict. One predictable stall on the button is the whole bargain.

⚠️ **A paint that changed no VALUE still changed PROVENANCE — fixed.** `set_collision`/`set_elevation` write a value, an explicit bit AND provenance, but `apply_edit`'s `changed` predicate only asked about the first two — so repainting a value that was already correct reported "nothing happened": no dirty flag, no undo entry, and a mutation that still reached disk on the next deliberate save. **Measured on a real editing pass: 8 of 38 authored cells arrived that way.** Silent was the problem, not the flip — an IMPORTED cell going AUTHORED is what makes the baker refuse a re-bake, so a gesture that changed nothing visible could quietly make a map un-re-bakeable. New `MapData.is_authored()` is the third clause, deliberately WIDER than `needs_review` (which now routes through it, so the provenance read lives in one place). `snapshot_cells()` had ALWAYS captured provenance — its own comment says a paint changes "a VALUE and an EXPLICIT BIT and possibly PROVENANCE" — so undo needed no change at all once the predicate was honest.

**Pallet Town reset to square 1.** Rob's editing pass was deliberately throwaway, so it was force-rebaked away (`map_baker.tscn -- PalletTown_Frlg --force`): 38 AUTHORED → 0, 5 review cells → 0, and the regenerated `.tres` came back **byte-identical to HEAD** and dropped out of `git status`, which also proves the baker is deterministic for that map. `--force` skips the divergence guard, so no scratch save ran and no new `SubResource` drift was introduced — `check_bake_diff --all` still reports 1 of 8, still `Route1_Frlg`.

✅ **The temporary diagnostics are GONE and painting persists again.** [This bullet previously read "TEMPORARY DIAGNOSTICS IN THE TREE — and they make painting non-persistent", warning that `save_map_data()` was commented out inside `restore_cells()`, that this was the only save call site, and that nothing worth keeping should be painted until a fix landed. All of that is now false and is corrected rather than left to alarm the next session.] Both `===== TEMPORARY DIAGNOSTIC (M27B step 1) =====` blocks are removed, `_pre_drag` is gone from the plugin, and persistence is the **Save Map Data** button's job with the dirty banner saying when it is owed — see the two paragraphs above. Verified by grep: zero diagnostic blocks anywhere in `scripts/overworld/`, `addons/map_overlay_editor/` or `scenes/overworld/`.

✅ **FIXED 2026-08-09 — see the strikethrough note below.** Clicking a marker now calls `EditorInterface.inspect_object(hit)`, which shows the entity's Inspector WITHOUT moving the scene-tree selection — so the MapOverlay stays the edited object, `_handles()` keeps answering, viewport input keeps arriving, and a second click cycles to the next entity on that cell. ⚠️ **The old code's REASONING was right and only its MECHANISM was wrong**: the selection genuinely must not grow a second node (it would show two inspectors and edit the wrong one); the mistake was solving that by moving the selection at all. That makes this the last of the three defects this surface has shipped — and, per its own note below, it stayed open precisely because the plugin has no automated coverage, which is still true: this fix is confirmed by reading, not by a test.

~~🔶 **KNOWN DEFECT — click-to-select disables itself.**~~ Clicking a marker selects the entity, which makes the plugin stop handling the edited object, so `next_in_stack`'s cycling is **unreachable through the UI**. One-line fix: `EditorInterface.inspect_object()` instead of mutating the selection. **The plugin is this project's one surface with no automated coverage, and this is the third defect to ship there** after the editor clip-rect bug and whatever the undo pass finds — which is why the cycle DECISION lives on the overlay as a pure function (`next_in_stack`) and the plugin retains only the act of selecting.

**Tests.** `m27a_step_resolver_test` **44 → 295/295** via `scripts/run_overworld_tests.sh` (which fails a run on engine ERROR lines); `EXPECTED_TOTAL` **190 → 294**, gated **90 → 104**, all re-measured from real runs per the MEASURED-CONSTANT RULE. New sections: **S** (map table, entity source, warp states), **T** (sight-line maths, all five no-ray states), **U** (click hit-test), **V** (stacks, cycle, counts — using the REAL corridor overlaps as fixtures), **W** (the baker guard's normalisation, driven against scratch strings, including a repointed `[ext_resource] path=` that over-broad normalisation would mask), **X** (the paint-gesture lifecycle plus the dirty invariant — X.18-X.24 pin the provenance-only repaint above, and that fixture has to stamp BOTH explicit bits like real imported data, because `_synth`'s empty `attr_explicit` would let the pre-existing not-explicit clause catch it by accident and the test would prove nothing). Fresh-checkout path verified balancing with both `.tscn` and `.tres` moved aside.

⚠️ **Section J no longer asserts against a live editing target.** J.04-J.06 read cell (0,0) and J.07 demanded an empty review backlog — both true only while nobody had used the tool, so one saved AUTHOR-mode paint turned the suite red for doing the intended thing, and the failure read like an importer regression. J.04-J.06 now probe the first genuinely imported cell (gating cleanly if none remains); **J.07 asserts the invariant that actually holds — the importer emits no half-decided cells** — by checking every untouched cell is explicit on both attributes, which is strictly stronger than an empty-backlog check for the imported subset AND survives any amount of painting; J.10 measures a DELTA against whatever the last save left. Confirmed before relying on it that `review_cells()` is a fresh row-major rescan returning a NEW array per call, so the baseline is a real snapshot and not a view the second call mutates underneath — that, rather than ordering, was the failure mode worth checking. Riders also landed: section H now gates on both artifacts rather than gating on the `.tres` while asserting on the `.tscn`, and H.13's comment no longer claims Pallet Town carries a hand-instanced `MapOverlay` — Section N asserts that no baked map does.

## M27N — Field weather *(new block, scoped 2026-07-31)*

**Scoped, not built.** Overworld weather, which no earlier M27 scoping pass named. Distinct from battle weather (`[M26B4]`), which is a separate system — source tears field weather down at battle entry (`Transition_StartIntro`), and `battle_bg.c` has zero weather references.

**Kanto uses 4 of the 24 weather constants, and the distribution is the scoping fact.** Measured across all 419 Kanto maps: `WEATHER_NONE` 331, `WEATHER_SUNNY` 62, `WEATHER_FOG_HORIZONTAL` 19, `WEATHER_SHADE` 7. No rain, sandstorm, snow, thunderstorm, ash, drought or cycles anywhere in the region. The 32-map corridor is **7 SUNNY** (every outdoor route/town), **1 SHADE** (Viridian Forest), 24 NONE (every interior).

⚠️ **There are ZERO weather coord events in Kanto.** Weather is a per-map-header field applied on map entry, full stop — `DoCoordEventWeather` has no Kanto consumer. Recorded because a scriptless coord event is the obvious place to look for weather and finding none there could easily read as an importer gap rather than as absence. **The importer currently captures the header's `weather` field not at all.**

**SUNNY and SHADE are PURE COLOUR GRADES — no sprites, no particles, no layers.** Both have an empty `_Main`, create nothing, and set only `targetColorMapIndex` (0 for Sunny, 3 for Shade) with `colorMapStepDelay = 20`, i.e. a gradual fade between indices rather than a snap (`field_weather_effect.c:156, 2256`). The mechanism beneath is a **19 x 32 per-channel lookup table** (`sDarkenedContrastColorMaps`, plus a sibling `sContrastColorMaps`, `field_weather.c:65/88`) remapping every 5-bit palette channel, applied across all 32 palettes via `ApplyColorMap(0, 32, index)`. **FOG_HORIZONTAL is the one Kanto type that is more than a grade** — same colour map plus a scrolling fog sprite layer with its own blend coefficients (`:1372`).

**Proposed sub-tiers.** **W1** — the importer captures `weather` into `MapData` (one field; `.tres`-only, so no `--force` re-bake, per C1's precedent). **W2** — port both 19x32 LUTs verbatim plus a LUT shader on a `CanvasLayer` above the world and below the message box; verbatim tables are standing practice here (`gSineTable`'s 320 entries, `EXP_SCALING_FACTORS`' 211). **W3** — apply on map entry with the real 20-frame-per-step fade, settling during the existing warp fade. **W4, DEFERRED** — FOG_HORIZONTAL's scrolling layer; **zero corridor consumers**, so it waits for the full-region import.

⚠️ **HONEST SIZING: the corridor's entire visible payoff is one map.** Colour map index 0 is very nearly the identity (`{0,0,1,2,3...29}` — it only compresses the top end), so SUNNY on those 7 maps is close to a no-op; the real delta is Viridian Forest looking darker, shade cutting brights ~19% against sunny's ~6%. Weather becomes genuinely worthwhile at full-region import (19 fog maps, 7 shade). **It is cheap, self-contained, blocks nothing, and can drop in at any pause** — it is not a prerequisite for anything.

**Three things to settle at build time rather than guess now**: which of the two 19x32 tables is selected and by what; faithful LUT shader versus a cheap `CanvasModulate` tint (recommend the LUT — similar cost, cannot drift from source); and whether overworld weather feeds battle weather, since `field_weather` IS referenced from `battle_main.c` and `battle_util.c` but that link was NOT traced and must not be assumed either way.

## M27P — Overworld polish *(new block, opened 2026-08-07 — OPEN AND EMPTY BY DESIGN)*

⚠️ **THIS BLOCK IS A REVIEW SURFACE FOR ROB, NOT A SCOPED WORK LIST.** It
exists so overworld polish candidates have one named home instead of
accumulating as loose notes in other blocks' headers. **Every item that lands
here is in/out by Rob's call** — nothing is committed to by being written
down, and any candidate may be cut outright, deferred, or moved to the block
that really owns it.

⚠️ **DELIBERATELY EMPTY — no speculative content added**, following **M25i**'s
own precedent in this file ("deliberately left open/unscoped, no speculative
content added"). A block opened with a pre-populated candidate list would read
to a later session as scope Rob had already approved, which is the exact
confusion the C/T/A key's `A` slot exists to prevent. Items are added by Rob,
or proposed to Rob and added only once he says so.

**Sequencing is not assumed.** `M27O` carries its own "sequenced at the end of
M27 at Rob's direction" note, so whether polish runs before or after whiteout
is an open question for Rob — not something this block's position in the file
decides.

**Standing rules apply unchanged**: the C/T/A key above governs any row this
block grows, and rule 1 — never fill `A` — is in force here as everywhere
else.


## M27Q — Entity & event authoring *(scoped 2026-08-08; Q1–Q4 BUILT the same day, Q5 deferred indefinitely)*

**Where this block stands, 2026-08-08.** `Q1` `Q2` `Q3` `Q4` are built and
green; **`Q5` is deferred indefinitely by Rob and is not scheduled work**. The
paragraphs below were written as a proposal and are kept as the scope of
record — where a decision was later taken, it is marked inline at the tier
rather than by rewriting the proposal around it.

⚠️ **THE `A` SLOT IS STILL EMPTY FOR ALL OF IT.** Built and tested is not
approved, and one thing in particular has NEVER BEEN SEEN IN PLAY: Q1 changed
every trainer's AI, leaving 1,205 of 1,477 measurably weaker, and no human has
fought one. That is the largest unverified claim in this block.

**Four things were found that were not in the plan**, each recorded at its own
tier: the `CHECKVIABILITY` importer bug (80 trainers silently missing a flag
this project already implements), the `@tool` placeholder bug (which made every
Q2/Q3 affordance on `TrainerData` invisible), the trainer-class dropdown
data-loss bug caught by its own coverage assertion, and the overlay
contamination footgun, now closed structurally rather than by discipline.

⚠️ **SCOPED BY RECON, ORIGINALLY AWAITING ROB'S IN/OUT CALL.** Nothing below is built and
nothing is approved. Every number is measured; the command that produced it is
named. The `A` slot stays empty here as everywhere else.

**Why its own block rather than sub-tiers of M27M.** M27M is authoring tooling
and this is authoring tooling, so folding in was the obvious move — but every
one of `M27M1`–`M27M7` and `M27M-T` is tile-, block-, or art-centric, and none
of them touches a placed entity. This block is entity-side throughout: NPCs,
trainers, scripts, dialogue. Kept separate so "which tier owns the tile grid"
and "which tier owns the things standing on it" never have to be disambiguated
by reading the sub-tier body. Rob's call, 2026-08-08.

**Modelled on HexManiacAdvance's object-event panel**, which unifies the event
struct, the script bytes and the trainer table behind one selection
(`deepwiki.com/haven1433/HexManiacAdvance` §9.4). ⚠️ **The port of that idea is
NOT a docked panel, and that is the block's main design decision.** HMA needs a
dock because a hex editor has no scene tree and no inspector — it invents the
whole selection-and-properties surface. This project has both, and
`plugin._select_entity_at` + `MapOverlay.next_in_stack` already resolve a click
into a selected node with stacked-entity cycling. A dock would re-solve
selection sync, multi-select, deselect and freed-node guards — code that does
not exist, on the project's one surface with no automated coverage. Everything
node-scoped goes in the **Inspector**; the two project-wide tools are buttons.

⚠️ **THE FIVE FIELDS SPLIT ACROSS THREE STORAGE CLASSES, AND THE SPLIT IS THE
PLAN.** `movement_type`, `trainer_key` and `script_label` are `@export`s on the
placed node — in-memory until the scene is saved, no `ResourceSaver`, and
Inspector undo already covers them. `ai_flags` and the team roster live in a
shared `data/trainers/<KEY>.tres` — a real disk write, and the one that pays
the `EditorFileSystem` rescan that cost the collision-paint path ~5 s per
stroke. Dialogue preview is derived and writes nothing. Sequencing the cheap
class first is what keeps `ResourceSaver` out of the block until Q4.

**Measured during scoping (2026-08-08), and three of these correct claims made
elsewhere in this file:**

- ⚠️ **`movement_range_x/y` IS already imported** — `gen_map_import.py:555-556`
  emits `range_x`/`range_y`, and `grep -h "^range_x = \|^range_y = "
  scenes/maps/*.tscn | sort | uniq -c` finds **218 non-zero values** across the
  32 baked maps. Any plan listing it as a data prerequisite is working from a
  stale premise.
- ⚠️ **`ScriptVM.describe()` cannot render a script.** It is a runtime state
  dump of a *running* VM (`pc`, `current_op`, `pause_reason`, …,
  `script_vm.gd:2251`). No static op-stream renderer exists anywhere. The data
  is already structured (`ops_by_label[label]` is `Array[{"op","args"}]`), so
  writing one is formatting work — but it is work, and `describe()` is not it.
- **Nothing validates `script_label`.** `OverworldEntity._get_configuration_
  warnings` checks only `elevation`; `NPC` adds `movement_type`, `TrainerNPC`
  adds `trainer_key`/`sight_range`. A misspelled script label is silent in the
  editor, silent at boot, and first surfaces as the VM halting `UNRESOLVED`
  mid-conversation.
- **The shared-`.tres` blast radius is effectively nil.** A python scan of
  `assets/maps/*.json` gives **432 trainer placements / 418 distinct keys / 14
  keys used twice / 0 spanning more than one map** — every duplicate is a
  same-map adjacent pair (`TWINS_KIRI_JAN` at Route14 (13,51)/(12,51), and 13
  more across `COOL_COUPLE`/`CRUSH_KIN`/`SIS_AND_BRO`/`YOUNG_COUPLE`), i.e.
  double-battle partners sharing one trainer record. Correct data, not a hazard.
  **No cross-map warning is owed.**
- **`data/map_scripts.json` holds 17,159 labels**, not the 17,137 this file
  quotes elsewhere, and is **8.6 MB** — so an in-editor validator must cache it
  statically, never parse per-validation.
- **Inspector customisation is available and entirely unused here.**
  `ClassDB.class_exists` confirms `EditorInspectorPlugin`/`EditorProperty` in
  4.7.1; `grep -rn "_get_property_list\|_validate_property\|EditorInspector
  Plugin\|@export_enum"` over `scripts`/`addons`/`scenes` returns **nothing**
  outside the vendored dialogue_manager.

**Sub-tiers, in dependency order.**

**Q1 — the `ai_flags` wiring fix.** ⚠️ **ASSIGNING AI FLAGS IS THEATRE UNTIL
THIS LANDS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FIRST AND WHY IT SHIPS ALONE.**
`TrainerAI.from_trainer_data()` exists and copies `ai_flags` correctly
(`trainer_ai.gd:98-100`) and has **zero non-test callers**; the single live
construction site is `battle_screen_shared.gd:1828-1829`, which does
`TrainerAI.new()` + `ai.tier = Tier.SMART` while `opp_trainer_data` sits
resolved six lines above and is handed to `set_trainer_data()` for money,
items and the intro banner but never for AI. ⚠️ **THIS IS NOT A STRICT
UPGRADE AND WANTS A DELIBERATE CALL.** Parsing every `.tres` gave, *as first
measured and BEFORE the importer fix two paragraphs down*, **1183 × `1`, 173 ×
`7`, 87 × `3`, 15 × `0`, 13 × `11`, 5 × `23`, 1 × `15`** = 1477. (Both
corrections below revise this; the figure is kept because the reasoning that
follows it is what found them.)
⚠️ **AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS BLOCK GREPPED `^ai_flags = ` AND MISSED THE 15
ZEROES** — Godot omits an `@export` sitting at its default when it writes a
`.tres`, so the absent line IS the value. Same grep-vs-measure failure as the
`OBJECT_EVENT` 26-vs-25 precedent; parse, do not grep.

⚠️ **AND ASKING "ARE WE MISSING AI OPTIONS?" FOUND A SECOND, REAL BUG — 80
TRAINERS WERE MISSING A FLAG THIS PROJECT ALREADY IMPLEMENTS.**
`gen_trainer_data.AI_TOKEN_MAP` had no `CHECKVIABILITY` key, and
